# The docket — findings that are real but are not this rung's

Created 2026-08-11 by the chief supervisor, as the queue **R-CONVERGE** requires.

A rung that absorbs every new finding never closes. A rung that DROPS them is worse.
This file is the third option: a finding made outside a rung's declared scope is written
down here, with the rung it surfaced in and what would settle it, and the rung closes.

**This is a queue, not an archive.** An item leaves it by being executed or by Katie
ruling it out — never by ageing.

Rules that govern entries here:

- Every item names **where it was found**, **what it would take to settle**, and **who
  owns it** (fleet / chief / Katie).
- Items filed under **R-DEPTH** (depth-3 meta-work) stay filed unless a depth-2 finding
  falsified something published. They are marked `[R-DEPTH — filed, not executed]`.
- Items filed under **R-VALUE** are the residuals of a rung that closed as PASS WITH
  RESIDUALS. They are marked with the rung.
- No item here is a claim about the world. Where an item asserts something, it carries
  its evidence or it says it has none yet.
- **IDs ARE APPEND-ONLY AND STABLE. NOTHING IS EVER RENUMBERED.** (Katie, W-4,
  2026-08-11.) A new entry takes a **fresh** ID — check the highest in use first. If two
  writers collide on a number, **both keep their text and the later one takes the next free
  ID**; the earlier one is not moved.

  **Renumbering is the defect, not the fix, and I learned that by doing it twice.** Three
  collisions happened in this file on 2026-08-11 — all from concurrent sessions that could
  not see each other — and I resolved two of them by renumbering: a second `D8` became
  `D8b`, and a grader's `D5`/`D6` became `D19`/`D20`. **Every reference written before that
  moment then pointed at the wrong row.** One was found the same day, in a document that had
  correctly refuted a claim and then rotted while sitting there being right. **An ID is a
  reference target; moving it breaks every citation you cannot see.** The collision costs one
  duplicate number. The renumbering costs every pointer in the corpus.

  **Superseded IDs are struck in place and kept, never reused** — a retired number stays
  retired so an old citation resolves to a withdrawal rather than to somebody else's finding.

  **ID map for the two renumberings already committed**, so references written before
  2026-08-11 resolve rather than rot. This map is permanent; it is not a cleanup step.

  | as originally cited | now | what it is |
  |---|---|---|
  | `D8` (the plateau-balance correction) | **`D8b`** | WITHDRAWN — refuted by D9 |
  | `D8` (B2's seven candidates) | `D8` — unmoved | the original holder of the number |
  | `D5` (worktree staleness, from grade round 5) | **`D19`** | filed by the V16 grader |
  | `D6` (no precision figure, from grade round 5) | **`D20`** | filed by the V16 grader |

## B. Machinery — each defect class becomes an executable check

A lesson without a check is a lesson that will recur. Owner: fleet.

> **CLAIM AN ITEM BEFORE YOU DISPATCH IT — write your session into the Status column first.**
> Added 2026-08-11 after two chief sessions independently dispatched the *same four briefs*
> off the same directive block. The B1 collision is the proof: two agents ran the identical
> sweep brief, and one's `Write` landed on the other's already-committed
> `docs/SWEEP_REFRAME_AUDIT.md`, replacing a 335-line document with a 281-line one and
> dropping two sections. It survived only because that agent went looking, compared both,
> judged the other strictly more complete, and restored it with `git checkout --`.
>
> **That is L-77 with commits instead of a scratchpad, and the failure is the dispatcher's,
> not the agent's.** This file is the one surface both sessions already read. One edit before
> dispatch makes the collision visible while it is still cheap — the agents do not exist yet.

this should take something else.

the three agents believed to have died mid-task had in fact landed its commit (`89c2a467`,

was never committed, and an uncommitted worktree is the one thing a second session's agent
can silently clobber.

## D. Rung residuals

Filled as rungs close under R-VALUE. A rung closing here does not mean these are small;
it means they are not what that rung declared it would settle.

| # | Item | Where found | What settles it | Owner |
|---|------|-------------|-----------------|-------|
| D1 | **A mutation test can report the exact opposite of the truth, via stale bytecode.** Verifying V16's E3 recompute, an equal-length source mutation (`(5, 5,` → `(4, 5,`) plus a restore left a stale `scripts/__pycache__/self_audit.cpython-312.pyc`: Python's source-based invalidation compares `(mtime, size)`, and `cp` back gave the same size. For three consecutive runs `sa._PLACE_REACH_B` was `(4, 5)` at run time while the file on disk read `(5, 5)`. The clean control **failed** and the mutated case **passed** — a perfectly inverted mutation matrix, which a less suspicious reading would have written up as "the recompute is dead". Re-run with the cache cleared before every step, the matrix is correct in all four cells. | V16 grade-four close-out, 2026-08-11 | A mutation-test harness that **clears `__pycache__` between every cell** — **NOT `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, see the correction below** — and that asserts the clean control and the mutated case in the *same* run so an inversion cannot look like a pass. Until it exists, every mutation result in this lab is only as good as whether its author happened to clear the cache. |
| **D1a** | **[CORRECTION 2026-08-11, chief — the fix D1 first recommended DOES NOT WORK, and I propagated it to three agents before testing it.]** `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` stops Python **writing** a `.pyc`; it does nothing about **reading** a stale one that already exists — and one always exists, because the module was imported before the mutation. Reproduced minimally: with a stale pyc present and an equal-length mutation, the mutated cell returns the **clean** value while the file on disk holds the mutation. **A wrong fix is worse than no fix: it converts an unverified result into a falsely verified one.** Executed mitigation table — deleting `__pycache__` before every cell: **WORKS**; `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` to a fresh dir per cell: **WORKS**; a mutation that changes byte length: works, but that is a property of the edit, not a guard; `sleep` **between the prime and the write**: works; `sleep` between the write and the run: **FAILS**, the mtime was already stamped; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`: **FAILS**. **Mechanism:** Python's timestamp invalidation compares `int(st_mtime)` — *whole seconds* — plus size, so a prime-mutate-run-restore-run cycle completing inside one second with a length-preserving edit matches both halves of the key. This is why the defect surfaces for scripted agents and essentially never for someone editing by hand. | Chief, on D1, 2026-08-11 | Nothing further — executed. The harness in D1 must be built against cache **clearing**; a harness built on the flag would certify the same false passes. | fleet | fleet |
| D2 | **Two published surfaces carry an underived count: "four" digit-form linear-algebra ranks.** `campaign/V16_GRADE.md` says *"swept over every tracked UTF-8 surface … four genuine linear-algebra ranks written as digits"*; `docs/PRODUCT_LIST.md:3765` repeats *"It is not — four are digits"*. Re-run mechanically (`git ls-files` piped to `/usr/bin/grep`, **not** this shell's `grep`, which is `ugrep --ignore-files`) the number is not four — it moves with whether the guard's own source, its tests, and the audit records that quote them are inside the filter. Neither surface states a filter or a commit, so neither can be reproduced. The count is not load-bearing — the exclusion was rewritten on the *shape* of the two senses, not on how many instances exist — which is why V16 dropped the figure from `self_audit.py` rather than restating it. The two published copies still carry it. | V16 grade-four close-out, 2026-08-11 | Either re-derive both figures with frame + filter + commit (B5/L-75/L-72), or replace them with the named paths, which do not drift. | fleet |
| D3 | **`docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md` holds the guard's two standing faults, and one of them is structurally unclearable.** Every run of `check_board_placement_words` reports WARN with exactly these two: a rule-A ordinal-three expression bound to the entrant the published board places second (a *quotation* of defect A, which the adjudication clause does not clear because the correcting sentence is too far away) and a rule-B margin-over-a-position-word comparison naming no holder (a quotation of defect C — and **rule B has no adjudication clause and cannot be given one**, since there is no correct form of a position word to sit beside a wrong one). *[DEQUOTED 2026-08-11 by the chief. Both faults were originally written here **verbatim**, and the V16 author round then found that two of the guard's four remaining live faults were **in this entry** — the docket row documenting the unclearable-quotation problem had become an instance of it. Asked directly, the guard bound the quoted ordinal to the entrant and flagged the quoted margin sentence, exactly as it should. The remedy is D3's own: state the shape, do not reproduce the string. This is the markdown analogue of the split-token convention `test_rank_claim_surfaces.py` already uses, and it is the third time tonight a record has been caught demonstrating the defect it describes.]* This is the check's permanent non-green floor: no future round can reach PASS while these stand, so a reader cannot tell this WARN from a new one. | V16 rounds 1–4; measured again at close-out, 2026-08-11 | Assemble the ledger's two quotations from split tokens the way `sdk/tests/test_rank_claim_surfaces.py` and `V16_AUTHOR_HELDOUT_SET.py` already assemble theirs — the convention exists and this surface predates it — **or** record an explicit dated accepted-exception so the WARN is legible as "the two known quotations, nothing new". | fleet |
| D4 | **The fixture-assembly convention is enforced by review alone, and review failed twice in one night.** `test_rank_claim_surfaces.py` states in its own header that a tracked file must not spell a wrong placement in full. The killed agent's inherited diff broke it, and so did my first draft of the repair: between us we put **eight** new real rule-A faults about a live entrant into the guard's own test file (measured: WARN 2 → WARN 10, all eight new ones in that one file). The stale-page risk is nonetheless real: if the unlink fails and the build then fails, an earlier mission's certificate stays on disk and the act says nothing about it either way. | B2 fail-open sweep, 2026-08-11 | Either route it through `workflows.withdraw_certificate` like its nine siblings, or record the deliberate decision that an act which makes no claim needs none. (Frame: `git ls-files '*.py'` piped to `/usr/bin/grep -ln "from an earlier mission"`, at `36c533f3`, excluding this rung's own test file which quotes the phrase to describe it. My first draft of this entry said **six**, from reading rather than counting, and the count was wrong — recorded here because an underived number in a docket entry about underived numbers is worth leaving visible.) ### D-follow-on — the two published results D1 puts in doubt

Filed by the chief, 2026-08-11, on the back of D1. **D1 says the instrument can lie. This
entry asks whether it already did, in something we published.**

A sweep of every commit in this tree since 00:00 today for mutation-proved claims returns
three hits, one spurious:

| Commit | Time | Claim | Why it carries D1's signature |
|---|---|---|---|
| `717d7e7a` | 06:19 | *"mutating `_PLACE_REACH` from 24 to 99 moves…"* | `24` → `99` is an **equal-length** edit — the size half of `(mtime, size)` is preserved by construction. |
| `6b37866a` | 07:14 | *"the recompute moves when I mutate my own sentences"* (V16 fourth grade) | Its own body states the file was **"restored byte-for-byte"**, which reproduces both halves of the invalidation key. |
| `d4509368` | 01:36 | *"the solves did not mutate their own inputs"* | **Not a mutation test** — a different sense of the word. Discarded, and recorded so the next sweep does not re-flag it. |

**Both live ones are POSITIVE results, and that is the dangerous polarity.** Each concluded
that a recompute is alive. Under the inversion D1 demonstrated, the module can hold the
mutated value at run time while the file on disk reads the original — so a test that appeared
to redden on mutation may have been reading stale mutated bytecode throughout, and the
recompute it certified as live may not be. That is a **false pass in a published claim**, not
merely an unverified one.

**What settles it:** re-run those two with `__pycache__` **cleared between every cell**
(NOT `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` — see D1a; the flag does not stop a stale pyc being read),
asserting all cells **in the same run** so an inversion cannot present as a pass. Routed to
the V16 agent with an explicit instruction not to re-open the rung for it.

**SETTLED 2026-08-11, V16 close-out — both hold, neither was a false pass.** Re-run in a
detached worktree at each claim's own commit, with `__pycache__` deleted before every cell,
each cell a fresh subprocess, and every cell reporting the value seen AT RUNTIME beside the
value ON DISK so an inversion would be visible rather than inferred. Both mutations were kept
**equal-length**, so both sat squarely in the vulnerable class rather than dodging it.

`717d7e7a`, `_PLACE_REACH` 24 → 99 (equal length), harness at `scripts/self_audit.py`:

| cell | expect | runtime | disk | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A clean | 24 | 24 | 24 | OK |
| B mutated 24→99 | 99 | 99 | 99 | OK |
| C restored byte-for-byte | 24 | 24 | 24 | OK |

The generated verdict line carried `24 of 45` in A and C and `99 of 45` in B. Claim holds.

`6b37866a`, one sentence in `V16_AUTHOR_HELDOUT_SET.py` mutated `Wu and Zhang` →
`Xu and Zhung` (equal length, one occurrence), test
`test_every_published_reach_figure_recomputes_from_its_sentences`:

| cell | expect | got | runtime has mutant | disk has mutant | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A clean control | GREEN | GREEN | False | False | OK |
| B one sentence mutated | RED | RED | True | True | OK |
| C restored byte-for-byte | GREEN | GREEN | False | False | OK |

Byte-for-byte restore verified by SHA-256. Runtime matched disk in every cell of both
matrices — no inversion. **D1 says the instrument can lie; these two say it did not lie
here.** D1 itself stays open: the finding is real and the harness it asks for is still not
part of `sdk/tests/`.

**The cheap answer that would settle it for free:** if either mutation was reverted with
`git checkout` rather than a copy, the mtime changes and the stale cache is defeated. One line
of evidence closes it.

**The general form, worth more than the two instances:** every mutation-proved result this lab
has produced is only as good as whether its author happened to clear the cache — and mutation
testing is the main way both live sessions have been proving that recomputes are live. Until
the harness in D1 exists, treat any mutation result produced without the guard as **unproven
rather than merely unverified.**
| D19-D20 note | *[RENUMBERED 2026-08-11 by the chief while landing the grade commit. These two were filed as **D5** and **D6** on a worktree branch cut when the docket ended at D4; `main` meanwhile grew D5-D18, so both numbers were taken. Kept and renumbered rather than merged or dropped -- **the third ID collision in this file today**, all three from concurrent writers who could not see each other. The docket needs allocated IDs, not sequential ones; filed as its own item.]* | | | |
| D19 | **A worktree can isolate a grader from the commit it was sent to grade, and R-ISOLATE's stated caveat does not cover it.** Grade round 5 was dispatched into a worktree whose branch was cut **18 commits behind** the subject: `HEAD` was `2c2c6a9a` (V15 round 5), and the V16 close-out it was sent to execute — `c05a03e0`, `236465a5`, `9c5b7b9d` — was not an ancestor of it (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 9c5b7b9d HEAD` → 1). The V16 work was simply absent from the checkout. R-ISOLATE part 1 already carries a caveat and it is about **gitignored** files; this is a second, independent way the same mechanism fails open, and it produces the identical shape — an honest, confident report about a tree that does not contain the subject. A grader that had gone straight to the files would have returned five UNEXECUTABLEs, or worse, five confident verdicts on the wrong revision. Detected only because `git log --oneline -3` disagreed with the dispatch brief's own quoted commit subjects; recovered with `git merge --ff-only 1393b8b4` inside the worktree. | V16 grade round 5, 2026-08-11 | Have the dispatcher **state the subject SHA in the brief**, and have the agent assert `git merge-base --is-ancestor <subject> HEAD` before it executes anything — the same "print what you can see before you report what you found in it" discipline the gitignore caveat already prescribes, applied to revision rather than to file count. | fleet |
| D20 | **The placement guard publishes four recall figures and no precision figure, and both of grade round 5's findings are precision defects.** `_PLACE_REACH` (three rows) and `_PLACE_REACH_B` all measure MISSES — how much placement language the patterns fail to reach — and the verdict line spends a long paragraph on them. Nothing measures the opposite direction: how often the guard binds an ordinal to an entrant in a sentence that is not a placement at all. The frame's blind-spot list names one over-binding mechanism (item 6, a surname that is also an ordinary English word) and it is not the mechanism either E2 or E4 fails by. A reader is given a well-quantified account of what the guard misses and no account of what it invents — and the guard's own comment argues the invented one is the worse of the two, "because the false positive is loud and the false negative is silent". | V16 grade round 5, 2026-08-11 | A held-out set of sentences that are NOT placements — linear-algebra ranks, dated history, quotations, cross-sentence adjacencies — measured the way the three recall sets are, recomputed from committed inputs, and interpolated into the verdict beside them. `campaign/V16_GRADE_ROUND5_PROBES.py` is a 20-sentence starting point with its controls already separated. | fleet |
| D21 | **The staleness assertion is unsatisfiable for any subject landed by cherry-pick, and it failed on the first attempt to clear it.** D19's remedy — assert `git merge-base --is-ancestor <subject> HEAD` before executing — was adopted, and the very next use of it was a false STALE. The V16 round-2 author asserted against subject `0a51269d`, got STALE, and stopped; the chief landed the commit as cherry-pick `df53488e` and dispatched "re-run the assertion; it will pass now". **It did not pass and it never will.** A cherry-pick replays the diff under a new SHA (`0a51269d`'s parent is `1393b8b4`; `df53488e`'s is `0049463f`), so the original commit is not an ancestor of `main` and cannot become one. The content is present and byte-identical — every file the two commits touch, plus the two files under repair, matched by blob hash, and the committed probes reproduced the recorded 12-disagree/8-control split with zero drift — while the ancestry proxy says absent. This is the same defect as the E2 finding one rung down: a proxy standing in for the property actually wanted, confidently wrong in both directions. Note the standing cost: **every future agent dispatched to subject `0a51269d` will halt**, because the identifier in the brief now names a commit no ancestry query on `main` can reach. | V16 fix round 2, 2026-08-11 | Two parts. (1) The assertion must be **content-addressed, not commit-addressed**: pair or replace ancestry with `git rev-parse HEAD:<path>` blob identity on the files under repair, plus execution of the subject's committed probes with a drift check — that is what established the position here when ancestry could not. (2) A dispatcher landing a grader's branch should **merge rather than cherry-pick** when the SHA has already been quoted in a brief, or re-quote the new SHA in the dispatch; otherwise the brief's own identifier is stale on arrival. | fleet |
| D22 | **A repair's comment states a cause that executing the build it describes contradicts, and the correction it makes to a previous grader is the wrong way round.** V16's round-6 fallback is justified by a comment saying the two false negatives round 5 filed *"stay silent with the linear-algebra discriminator answering correctly"* and that *"distance was not what muted them"* — a correction of round 5's stated cause. Executed against `1393b8b4`, the build round 5 actually graded, `_place_linalg_subject` returns **True** for both shapes, which is the `continue` that discards the placement before the bind loop is ever reached. The discriminator was what muted them; round 5 was right. The correction is true only of the repaired build, where the discriminator now answers correctly and the distance test takes over — which is a statement about the fix, presented as a statement about the defect. The repair itself is needed either way, confirmed by the same execution, and the two figures quoted in the correction (69 and 73) reproduce exactly under the frame that governs, name-start to ordinal. The pattern is the one round 4 already met: a comment explaining why the code is correct, where the explanation is what is wrong. | V16 grade round 6, 2026-08-11 | A convention that any comment asserting **why a previous verdict was wrong** cites the SHA it was executed against and the value it observed, in the same sentence — the numbers here carried a frame and the causal claim carried none. Cheap to satisfy: both facts above came from one script that imports the old and new module side by side and prints the intermediate predicate, not just the verdict. | fleet |
| D23 | **Whitespace collapse makes markdown structure invisible to every clause-boundary rule in the placement guard, so "cannot cross a sentence" means only "cannot cross `[.!?;:]`".** `_placements` flattens the surface with `re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text)` before any boundary test runs, and every boundary rule downstream — `_PLACE_CLAUSE` for the subject-NP cut, `_PLACE_SENTENCE` for the bind probe — is a punctuation rule. A markdown heading, a list item and a table cell all end a unit of meaning **without terminal punctuation**, so after flattening they are not boundaries at all and the guard reads across them as if the text were one sentence. This corpus is predominantly markdown, which makes the unpunctuated boundary the ordinary case here rather than the exotic one. Executed at `72609a04`: three shapes bind an ordinal to an entrant named in the previous structural unit at gaps of 65, 69 and 54 characters against a `_PLACE_BIND` of 40, and the identical text with a full stop inserted is correctly silent. | V16 grade round 6, 2026-08-11 | Either flatten to a **boundary-preserving** representation — substitute a sentinel for a blank line, a heading, a list marker and a cell separator before collapsing the rest, and add that sentinel to `_PLACE_CLAUSE` and `_PLACE_SENTENCE` — or state in the frame that the guard's unit is the punctuation-delimited clause and not the sentence, so the written claim matches what runs. The first is a few lines and testable; the second is free and honest. | fleet |
| D24 | **The bind's boundary probe cannot tell an abbreviation-final period from a sentence-final one, and both the old and the new form get one of the two cases wrong.** `_PLACE_SENTENCE` fires on `[.!?]` + optional closers + whitespace + an uppercase-or-opening character. Until `72609a04` the probe appended the real next character, so a period followed by a digit or a lowercase token was not treated as a boundary — the defect grade round 5 filed, six shapes binding straight across a full stop. The repair appends a constant that is unconditionally uppercase, which fixes all six and, by the same stroke, makes **every** period-plus-space a boundary, including the one closing an abbreviation. Executed old-vs-new in one invocation with `__pycache__` cleared first: an abbreviation followed directly by an ordinal, and by an ordinal-plus-noun, faulted at `1393b8b4` and are silent at `72609a04`. Both are real wrong placements, now missed. The direction is the cheap one, but the comment making the trade says the appended character's identity *"was never doing any work"*, and on this class it was doing exactly one job. | V16 grade round 6, 2026-08-11 | A boundary test with a short abbreviation exception — the closed set that actually occurs in this corpus (`et al.`, `Fig.`, `Eq.`, `Sec.`, `No.`, `cf.`, `e.g.`, `i.e.`, `vs.`) checked against the token immediately before the period — plus a two-direction control set beside the existing over-fix controls: shapes that must still bind after an abbreviation, and shapes that must stop binding after a sentence. The current controls only test one of the two directions, which is why the trade landed unnoticed. | fleet |
| D25 | **Two boundary rules run inside one function and they disagree about what a boundary is, so a semicolon stops the fallback and does not stop the ordinary bind.** `_place_subject_np` cuts the subject phrase at `_PLACE_CLAUSE`, which after this round is `[.!?;:\|\n]`. The `_PLACE_BIND` loop twenty lines above it tests `_PLACE_SENTENCE`, which reads only `[.!?]`. So the SAME surface, with the SAME two words at the SAME distance, is a boundary for one rule and not the other, purely by which of the two paths the ordinal happens to take — and which path it takes is decided by whether the entrant sits inside 40 characters. Found by the precision set built for this round, not by a probe aimed at it: an ordinal with a position word, an entrant named before a semicolon and inside the bind, binds across the semicolon and faults. It is one of the twenty false FAULTs the published figure now counts, in class `cross-sentence`. **Not fixed here, and the reason is scope, not merit** — R-CONVERGE bounded this round to four deliverables and widening the bind's boundary test is a fifth behaviour change, which is precisely the "one more discriminator" move the ruling that governs this round exists to stop. | V16 round-6 close-out, 2026-08-11 | Give both rules the same boundary predicate — most cheaply by having the bind loop consult `_PLACE_CLAUSE` for the clause-level marks and keep `_PLACE_SENTENCE` for the sentence-level ones — and measure the result on the precision set AND all four recall sets in the same invocation, since a wider boundary buys precision with recall and this rung has already traded one for the other twice without measuring both ends. | fleet |
| D26 | **A test whose whole subject is "the set must be derived, not listed" enumerated its own evidence files by hand, and the sixth file added was the one that needed it.** `test_the_evidence_files_are_not_themselves_corpora_of_faults` guards the committed held-out sets against becoming corpora of the very defects they describe (docket D4). It named five files in a literal tuple. The file added this round was written with one position word unsplit, and the guard reported that source as carrying a real fault — caught, but caught by the pre-commit sweep the brief mandates rather than by the test, and a seventh file added by an agent who does not edit the tuple would be unguarded with the suite still green. This is the identical shape as the defect the whole test file was created for, quoted from its own docstring: *the list was the defect.* Fixed in the same commit by globbing the evidence directory with a floor on the count, so a set that vanishes fails rather than passes vacuously. Filed anyway, because the finding is that **a lab can restate a principle in a docstring and violate it eleven hundred lines below**, and the general form is worth more than the instance. | V16 round-6 close-out, 2026-08-11 | Executed for this instance. The general form is a sweep for *literal collections of paths inside tests whose stated property is derivation* — there is no reason to think this file is the only one, and the two places already found (the original `check_rank_claim_surfaces` surface list, and this) were both found by accident rather than by looking. | fleet |
| D27 | **The precision denominator that closes V16 is single-sourced, and its "WORST CASE" framing is falsified by the first independent set built against it.** The rung's closing condition is a published precision figure; the figure is 20 of 41 (49%) on a non-placement set built by the guard's own author *with the pattern list in hand*, and the generated verdict paragraph calls it "a WORST CASE on hard sentences". Grade round 7 built a disjoint 43-sentence set blind -- author's set and `_place*` patterns both unread until after the sentences were written -- and applied the author's own admission rule unchanged (only sentences a rule-A pattern actually matches enter the denominator; 18 of 43 excluded). Result: **19 of 25 (76%)**, Fisher exact two-sided **p = 0.040**, Wilson 95% intervals 57-89% against 34-64% -- the independent LOWER bound sits above the author's point estimate. Split by provenance: the four classes the ruling itself names went 9 of 15 (60%); the seven classes the grader invented went **10 of 10 (100%)**. So "worst case" is not a bound, it is an author's-set case, 27 points optimistic -- a false written claim of exactly the kind L-76 exists for, and the fourth such absolute this rung has produced. The lab already solved this one screen higher: `_PLACE_REACH` publishes THREE reach rows, each naming who built it and whether they were blind, under a note that "two honest sets disagree by twenty points". `_PLACE_PRECISION` publishes one row from the least independent possible source. | V16 grade round 7, 2026-08-11, subject `64281b6b` | Make `_PLACE_PRECISION` a multi-row table shaped like `_PLACE_REACH`, carrying the grader's row with `blind=True` beside the author's `blind=False`; delete "a WORST CASE" and let two disagreeing rows say what one row cannot. The set is committed at `campaign/V16_GRADE_ROUND7_PRECISION_SET.py` and recomputes, so it cannot go stale (L-79). **General form, worth more than the instance:** any figure this lab publishes about its own cost should carry the provenance of the sample it was measured on, because the sample's author is a variable in the result. **EXECUTED 2026-08-11 by V16 round 8, `5e4a0cc5`.** `_PLACE_PRECISION` is a two-row table shaped like `_PLACE_REACH`; both rows name their builder and their blindness and both recompute from committed sentences; the verdict computes the 27-point spread and which row is higher rather than asserting either; and "a WORST CASE on hard sentences" is withdrawn from both places it stood, quoted beside its falsification (L-76) and added to `_FALSIFIED` so reinstating it as a claim reddens the suite. | fleet |
| D28 | **The test that enforces the "knowingly accepted" proviso is scoped to the author's own set, so it cannot see a shape the author never thought of.** The V16 ruling permits a false-FAULT shape to stand unfixed PROVIDED it is counted in the precision figure and named in the blind-spot enumeration, and `test_every_false_fault_class_is_counted_and_named` enforces that well: dict equality in both directions plus a sum, so a shape cannot be counted in the figure and left out of the list. | D29 | **A gate can be non-monotone in the property it names, and G2 is: it awards its worst possible score to a perfectly localised correction.** `np.quantile(dev, 0.9)` returns **0.0** when fewer than 10% of cells deviate, so `dev >= thr` selects the whole domain and the top-decile share collapses to the window's base rate. Executed on the real mesh (21,000 cells, window 1,773 = 8.44%): pure noise confined **entirely inside the window** scores **8.4% and FAILS**, while the same noise smeared over the 14.14% W2 support scores **59.5% and PASSES**. Negative control passed -- noise over the whole domain returns 8.3%, so the instrument can return the null. **This did not cause the recorded 26.9%** (both real beta fields deviate in nearly every cell and sit far from the collapse); it is independent proof that the metric is invalid, not the explanation of the value. **General form:** any gate of the shape "fraction of the top q-quantile falling inside region R" silently inverts when the support of the deviation is smaller than the quantile, which is exactly the regime a *localisation* gate is supposed to reward. | Closure task 2, 2026-08-11, subject `64281b6b`, evidence `demo-output/website/campaign/CLOSURE_STAGE1_AND_C2_STATUS.md` §1.5 at `e43084c8` | A quantile-based spatial gate declares its behaviour when the deviating set is smaller than the quantile, or uses a base-rate-free enrichment statistic instead. The S1-with-priors pre-registration is the next place this choice gets made, and it must be made **before** that line's first compute (§2b). | fleet |

There is **no limit on scoring calls and there cannot be one**; the only published rule is the strict prohibition on *training or validating* on test cases. `CLOSURE_CHALLENGE_SUBMISSION_DRAFT.md:169-172` already states this correctly, but the constraint is recited elsewhere in a register that reads as an organiser requirement. **Naming it as a choice is worth more than obeying it silently:** a stricter-than-required protocol is a credibility asset only if the record says it was elective. | Closure task 2 submission-policy research, 2026-08-11, subject `64281b6b` | Every surface that recites the scoring-call count labels it as lab discipline rather than benchmark rule, per the §6a principle that a verdict carries what it was checked against. | fleet |

This entry records that the questions exist and are unanswerable from documents -- it does not authorise asking them. | Katie |
| D32 | **The L-76 enforcer cites thirteen tests, none of which exists, and its own missing-check class is structurally unable to fire on it.** `scripts/check_absolutes.py` backticks 13 distinct `test_*` names under the literal word *"Evidence:"*. **0 of the 13 resolve to a `def` in any `.py` on disk** (frame: `find` under the repo root, `.git` and `.claude/worktrees/` excluded; 1,459 real test defs found in the same sweep, so the finder works). Worse, the module's stated rule -- *"a cited test that does not exist is reported as its own class, `CITES_MISSING_CHECK`, and is counted as unbacked"* -- **does not hold in its own code**: `classify_unit` tests `elif resolved:` **before** `elif dangling:`, so a single runnable command anywhere in the same prose unit launders every dangling name into `BACKED`. Executed on the module itself: **BACKED 15, CITES_MISSING_CHECK 0**. Injection control, both directions: the unit *"This never fails. Evidence: `test_does_not_exist_anywhere_at_all`."* classifies `CITES_MISSING_CHECK`; the same unit with `python3 scripts/check_absolutes.py --frame tracked` appended classifies `BACKED`. The module's `resolved` list for its own docstring is `['<command>', 'path.py:function']` -- and `path.py:function` is the metasyntactic placeholder from its own prose. | V15 round 6, 2026-08-11, subject `2266c4e3` | Either write the 13 tests or delete the citations; and reorder the verdict cascade so `dangling` is consulted before `resolved`, with a test that plants a dangling name beside a runnable command. Until then every `BACKED` this checker reports is unsafe, and B3a's published 74% false-positive rate was measured on `UNBACKED` only -- `CITES_MISSING_CHECK` has no measured rate at all. **EXECUTED AND CLOSED 2026-08-11, commit `89c2a467`, subject `f6b4eec0`.** Both halves done, and the second half was done per citation rather than wholesale. **(1) The cascade is reordered** -- `dangling` is asked before `resolved` -- and the mixed unit is DECIDED rather than left implicit: **any dangling name makes the unit `CITES_MISSING_CHECK` even when a sibling citation resolves.** The argument is written at the branch and it is about the reader, not the arithmetic: someone who spot-checks one of five citations and finds it good does not check the other four, so one real name beside four fictional ones misleads him more than no citation at all. The rejected alternative (report the dangling ones as a note) is named there too, with its reason -- a note is not a verdict and nothing downstream counts it. The cost is stated: this is strictly the more severe rule, so the reason string now says `mixed unit: N other citation(s) here do resolve, and that does not rescue it`. **(2) Of the 13 citations, 11 were WRITTEN and 2 STRUCK.** Written into `sdk/tests/test_absolute_claims.py` (23 tests, 8 classes) because the claim beside each is load-bearing: the frame is derived from `git ls-files` rather than enumerated, the verdict is three-valued with skips counted per reason, backing is unit-level, the four gates each reject with a positive control surviving, the measured rate is recomputed from the fixture at call time and replays through the same `classify_unit` the corpus sweep uses. Struck where the name was aspirational, with the reason left in place at each site: the one asserting the audit's published rate matched the recomputed one (it does not -- **D50**), and the one asserting the sample was drawn from the checker's own output (a past sampling act at `8ebe2b8e` that no execution today repeats). **Neither struck name is repeated in the module even as a corpse**, since by its own rule a name resolving to nothing reads as backing. Also fixed: `FILE_FUNC_RE` was reading the metasyntactic placeholder `path.py:function` out of the module's own prose as a citation -- now written `<path>.py:<function>` (see **D51**). **NONE OF THE 13 WAS A DELETED TEST.** `git log --all -S"def <name>" -- '*.py'` returns zero commits for all thirteen and `sdk/tests/test_absolute_claims.py` had never existed in history, so they were fictional rather than decayed -- a different finding from a citation that rotted. **SELF-CLASSIFICATION, `--paths scripts/check_absolutes.py`, corpus as it stood at each cell:** BEFORE (code and prose as committed at `8a8b5392`) **BACKED 15, CITES_MISSING_CHECK 0, UNBACKED 2**, with **13 distinct dangling names silently discarded**; **ORDERING FIX ALONE**, same prose, same corpus: **BACKED 0, CITES_MISSING_CHECK 15, UNBACKED 2** -- the instrument indicting itself, which is the proof the fix reaches; AFTER (fix + citations settled) **BACKED 15, CITES_MISSING_CHECK 0, UNBACKED 2, 0 dangling**. **MUST-NOT-MATCH CONTROLS (L-84) AT TWO SCALES.** Unit: a unit whose every citation resolves is `BACKED`, and a unit citing only a runnable command is `BACKED` -- so the fix is not "flag everything". File: `UNBACKED` stays 2 and the verdict on itself stays `FLAGGED`, asserted by a test -- so the fix is not "switch it off". The two survivors are real and left standing (*"Every word the docket names has a family here"*, *"never enumerated by path"*). **AND THE CONTROL THAT DECIDES WHAT THE EVIDENCE IS.** Once the citations are real, the OLD order gives the SAME file-level counts as the new one, so **the file count no longer discriminates between fixed and unfixed** and cannot be the proof. The discriminating evidence is the unit-level mutation replay -- control and mutant loaded in ONE invocation with `__pycache__` purged -- where **exactly 2 of the 23 tests fail on the pre-fix order and 0 on the fix**. The two are the ones planting a dangling name beside a runnable command and beside a resolving test; the other 21 pass on both, which is what a test that pins prose rather than mechanism should do. **Not touched, on purpose:** no matching retuned, no gate added, no corpus sweep re-run, and the 74% figure not chased -- that is B3a/B3b, and two things found in passing are filed to D50 and D51 rather than fixed here. | fleet |

| D49 | **The two precision rows are built by two different admission rules, and the 27-point spread the verdict publishes is mostly that difference rather than the builder's.** `_place_precision_sentence` asserts, in the generated verdict and in BASIS, that *"a set's builder is a variable in the result, **the two here differ in that variable and in nothing else**"* and that *"**the same admission rule applies to both**, so neither denominator can be padded with sentences the guard never looks at."* Two different predicates implement that one rule. All 13 are true negatives, so the author's numerator stays 20. **Score both samples under ONE rule and the headline moves: raw-match everywhere gives 49% vs 73%, a 24-point spread (Fisher two-sided p = 0.075); `_placements` everywhere gives 71% vs 76%, a 5-point spread (p = 0.763).** The published pairing — the *looser* rule on the author's set, the *stricter* one on the grader's — is the only one of the four combinations that is not internally consistent, and it is the one that maximises the gap and yields the p = 0.040 the ruling at `02e941c4` cited. **What this does NOT touch:** both rows recompute correctly from their own sentences; all four recall figures are unchanged; the false-FAULT enumeration and its per-class counts are unaffected; and the struck *"worst case on hard sentences"* stays struck, because 76% exceeds the author's rate under **every** common rule. What is falsified is the comparison narrative and the absolute carrying it. | V16 grade round 9, 2026-08-11, subject `e16d7329` (ancestry OK; four-path content diff against HEAD `be36e22f` empty); executed at `be36e22f`, suite 111 passed | **Material — this is the rung's fifth L-76 absolute and it sits in the generated verdict, the surface an outside reader reads.** Settlement is narrow and needs no fifth discriminator: pick ONE admission predicate, apply it to both samples, recompute both rows from it, and **replace "differ in that variable and in nothing else" with the measured sensitivity** — the spread is 5 to 24 points depending on the rule, and that range is itself the honest statement. If the raw-match rule is kept, say why a sentence the discriminator correctly clears belongs in a precision denominator; if `_placements` is kept, say why a correct silencing should not be counted as a precision success. | D52 | **The repair that fixed E2's fail-open `.done` records moved the corpus denominator, and five published figures still cite the old one.** E2 diagnosed four zero-byte `.done` records — a zero-byte record being indistinguishable from a completed one to anything testing existence rather than content — and instructed that the four empties be *"marked, not deleted"*. That instruction was followed correctly: the four are present as `f6b2_{coarse,medium,fine,veryfine}_20260805T171046Z.done.INTERRUPTED`. **Nobody re-checked what the rename did to every consumer that counts.** `scripts/dispatch_queue.py:114` enumerates the corpus as `REGISTRY.glob("*.done")`, and `*.done` does not match `*.done.INTERRUPTED`, so the live count is **142**. `docs/PRODUCT_LIST.md` cites a **146**-record corpus in five places, including a measured statistic — *"Of 146 completion records, 3 carry a rank declaration"* — and *"all 146 records intact and unmodified"*, which is now false in the letter: four were renamed. **Measured 2026-08-12 at `d1585e3b`:** 142 `*.done`, 4 `*.done.INTERRUPTED`, **0 zero-byte, 0 `.partial`** — so the atomic-collector repair itself held, and this row is about the accounting, not the fix. **The registry is gitignored, so no repo-scoped sweep can see any of this** (L-75); it was found only by listing the directory directly. | state reconciliation after the 2026-08-12 outage, executed at `d1585e3b` | **This is the D50 shape a second time: a published denominator and a recomputed one that are two different samples, with no check that they agree.** Settlement is a choice, not a merge — decide whether the corpus is 146 records of which 4 are interrupted, or 142 usable records, then make every surface and every glob say the same thing. Whichever is chosen, the *interrupted* four must stay countable, because they are evidence of a real interruption and a corpus that silently drops them cannot answer how often collection fails. The general form worth more than the instance: **a fix that renames or re-scopes an artifact changes every consumer that counted it, and consumers are the half nobody re-runs.** **CLEARED 2026-08-14 by [OPUS-B], pinned at `8d977d23`, surfaces annotated at `975f3a8b` and `1c455160`. Three of this row's own claims were falsified by execution and are struck-and-kept here rather than corrected in place (L-76).** **~~"five published figures"~~ — the count was wrong by a factor of four.** A two-pass sweep (`git grep` for tracked files, plus a gitignore-blind `find`+`grep -lIE` pass reaching `solve_registry/`, `wall/`, `dist/certonomous-demo/`, `mission-output/` and `chief-engineer-runs/`, plus per-member `unzip -p` over the bundle and `pdftotext` over all four PDFs) found **~20 instances across 10 tracked files**, not five in one: `docs/PRODUCT_LIST.md` (5), `demo-output/website/campaign/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_2026-08-11.md` (4), `docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md` (3), `scripts/case_preflight.sh` (2), `docs/standards/INFRA_FAMILY_SUPERVISION_GUIDELINES.md` (1), `docs/LEDGER_HEADLINE_AUDIT.md` (1), `scripts/launch_solve.sh` (1), the live proposal `agenda/proposals/cross-family-lesson-propagation-sweep-within-one-week.json` (1), `sdk/tests/fixtures/absolute_claims_labelled.json` (1), and this docket's own E2 row (1). **~~"146 records of which 4 are interrupted, or 142 usable"~~ — neither is the number of completion records.** A **fifth** record, `f5a_re2000_20260729T021614Z.done.FAILED_ATTEMPT_cwd_bug`, was renamed out of the glob at 2026-07-29 02:19:53 (ctime, read off the file), before any published figure was written. Executed at `8d977d23`: **142 usable `*.done`, 5 renamed out (4 INTERRUPTED + 1 FAILED_ATTEMPT), 147 in all**, 0 zero-byte `*.done`, 0 `.partial`. So the historical 146 had ALREADY silently omitted a failed run, and **no surface in the repo has ever cited 147**. **~~"five published figures still cite the old one"~~, read as five errors — they were TRUE WHEN WRITTEN.** The four `*.done.INTERRUPTED` renames happened at **2026-08-11 17:31:31** (ctime); the two rank-retrospective entries are dated 2026-08-10 and the launcher-test entry 2026-08-11. At the moment each was written `*.done` really did return 146. The defect was therefore never a wrong digit but **the absence of any note recording the supersession**, which is why all five carry dated supersession notes and not one had its number overwritten — rewriting 146 to 142 would have destroyed a true record of what the registry held on 2026-08-10. **RE-DERIVED, NOT PATCHED:** the measured statistic was recomputed through the live code path — **3 records carry a rank declaration, and it is 3 over 142 AND 3 over 147**, so the "measured zero" finding survives either denominator; the companion 143 becomes 139 usable / 144 of all. **GENERATOR PINNED AT THE HEAD OF THE CHAIN, and the chain was shorter than expected: no generator held the literal at all.** 146 existed only as frozen prose; the only thing that ever produced the denominator was `scripts/dispatch_queue.py:114` itself. That glob now enumerates sibling classes positively via `*.done.*`, reports `records` / `excluded_from_records` / `excluded_by_class` / `all_completion_records` **separately and never summed**, and names a class it has never seen rather than dropping it — control at `8d977d23` over a synthetic registry containing `*.done.NEW_CLASS_NEVER_SEEN` returned 3 usable / 2 excluded / 5 total and an absent registry returned zeroes without raising (that control proves the enumeration logic ONLY, not anything about the live registry — L-84). The counts are deliberately not merged: a spend audit wants the 142, and *"how often does collection fail?"* is answered only by the 5 excluded, which is the exact reason E2 required them kept. **THE LIVE DEFECT THIS ROW DID NOT NAME, and the one that mattered most:** `scripts/launch_solve.sh` recorded that **proposal P6 names a 146-record corpus replay as a prerequisite, whose stated point was that four of the records it would read are empty**. Those four are the renamed four. A P6 replay written against `*.done` would read 142, **silently skip all four, and return clean having never touched the evidence it was commissioned to examine** — a prerequisite inverted into a blind spot by a correct repair, with no symptom. Annotated in the script at `1c455160` with the requirement that any replay glob `*.done*` and report classes separately. **LEFT OPEN, with reasons:** four surfaces were NOT edited — `docs/standards/INFRA_FAMILY_SUPERVISION_GUIDELINES.md`, `docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md` and `docs/LEDGER_HEADLINE_AUDIT.md` were held back under the standing-records constraint (H6) and need their owner; and `sdk/tests/fixtures/absolute_claims_labelled.json:1264` must NOT be edited at all — it is frozen labelled ground truth with a `drawn_at_commit`, and silently editing a labelled sample to match a corrected world is the D50 defect exactly. The live proposal `cross-family-lesson-propagation-sweep-within-one-week.json` rests its cost basis on the 146 denominator and is also open. **REACH THIS CLEARANCE CANNOT HAVE:** the registry is gitignored (L-75), so every figure above came from listing the directory directly and none of it is reproducible from a clean clone; `registry_attribution()` returns zeroes there rather than a wrong number. | fleet |
| D53 | **A repair for the gate that powers this box off was written, committed to the tree, and never installed; the machine ran the broken file for thirteen days.** `/usr/local/bin/auto-stop.sh` (root cron, every 5 min) decided *"is anyone working?"* by matching command-line **strings**, one being the literal `Certonomous/sdk`. A verification suite launched from the repo root as `python -m pytest sdk/tests/...` spells that path *relatively*, so it never matched and read as an idle box. **Executed 2026-08-12:** the gate powered the machine off at 02:45 and again at 15:35, the second time mid-suite, killing the tmux server and two in-flight verification runs. The same predicate failed the other way too — it matched *any* command line containing the pattern, including a `grep Certonomous/sdk` typed to investigate this very bug, which silently reprieved the box. **A test that misses the real thing and fires on a mention of itself is not a test.** The class was already known: memory recorded the predecessor incident of 2026-07-30, a backup `auto-stop.sh.orig-20260730` was taken, and a repaired version was staged at `scripts/auto-stop.sh.proposed` dated Jul 30 15:01. **`diff` proves the live script on 2026-08-12 was byte-identical to the backup**, so nothing was ever applied. | found while answering Katie's *"make sure the instance stops dying"*, executed and repaired at `025d59a6` | **Repaired at the gate, not worked around.** The three predicates now used are ones a spelling cannot change: process NAMES via `pgrep -x`; any worker whose CWD is inside the repo **and** which is burning CPU (the CPU sample is load-bearing — `chief_engineer.server` is a permanent daemon with a repo CWD, and counting mere presence would pin the box forever and delete the cost control the script exists for); and a Claude session transcript written inside the window. Cost discipline is unchanged; only the definition of *idle* moved. `sdk/tests/test_autostop_gate.py` asserts the installed gate has not drifted from the tracked one and exercises **both** directions — still stops a genuinely idle box, does not stop one whose transcript is seconds old — all five mutation-proved individually with `__pycache__` purged before every cell. **The transferable finding is not the regex.** It is that a fix living only in the repo was never checked against the artifact actually running, and nothing in the lab was watching that gap. Every piece of machinery this lab depends on that lives OUTSIDE the tree — cron entries, installed scripts, systemd units — has the same unguarded gap today. | fleet |
| D54 | **Rule B's fault message says "without naming who holds it"; the rule actually tests only whether a name sits INSIDE the phrase, and has no adjudication clause at any distance.** `_place_unnamed` (`scripts/self_audit.py`) is a bare regex over whitespace-flattened text. Rule A has `_PLACE_ADJUDICATED` = 400 characters and a clause that clears a wrong ordinal adjudicated nearby; **rule B has neither, and its own docstring says so** (*"rule B has no adjudication clause"*) — a deliberate and defensible choice about WIDENING the match, which nonetheless leaves the fault MESSAGE claiming more than the rule tests. **Executed 2026-08-12:** `"Yang leads at 0.0580. Our margin over the leader is 0.0027."` faults; `"Yang: our margin over the leader is 0.0027."` — the holder's name one character away — faults; only moving the name inside the phrase (`"our margin over Yang"`) clears it. **Consequence for how this corpus was measured:** of the 13 live occurrences at `5c9c63fb`, 9 named Yang within ±300 characters and 4 did not (`benchmarks.json`, `wall/wall.json`, `latex/closure_challenge_report.tex` second occurrence, and `sdk/scripts/build_benchmarks.py`) — but **that split is a property of the corpus, not of the guard**, which faults all 13 identically. A ±300 window measured over the same corpus put the anonymous count at 3 and missed the generator, which is the one that writes two of the others. | P1.2, 2026-08-12, executed against `5c9c63fb` | **NOT fixed here, deliberately: retuning a detector so that its author's own prose passes is how a guard gets tuned to a pretty number.** All 13 were repaired in the prose instead. What is open is whether the MESSAGE should be narrowed to what the rule tests, or the RULE widened with a proximity-clearing clause. **The second is not free and must be costed before it is built:** rule B's reach, as measured at `5c9c63fb`, rests on a five-sentence smoke test — the check's own verdict calls that "not a reach measurement" — and both precision rows published at `5c9c63fb` are rule-A sets, so there is no sample in this lab against which a rule-B widening could be shown not to lose the anonymous cases it exists to catch. The message is the cheaper and more honest of the two. **EXECUTED AND CLOSED 2026-08-14, commit `d2d6bd6c`** -- recovered uncommitted from the weekly-usage-limit kill that ended the fleet earlier that day, and verified by execution before it was taken. **The message was narrowed; the rule was not widened**, which is the choice this row recommended. The rule half is now **D77**, not the D63 the work cited: that ID was reserved by CITATION while the work sat uncommitted, and the fleet allocated it to an unrelated auto-stop defect hours before the work was recovered -- a pointer into an append-only register is sound only once the row exists. **RE-EXECUTED RATHER THAN BELIEVED, and it moved a number in this row.** Against a worktree of `5c9c63fb` itself, rule B has **14** occurrences there, not 13. The numerator reproduces exactly -- **9** name Yang within +/-300 characters -- and all four non-Yang surfaces named above are confirmed, but `docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md`'s *"Our margin over the runner-up"* is an uncounted fifth: the only `runner-up` phrase where the other thirteen are `leader` phrases, which is the likely reason it was missed. **This row's 13 stands as the dated measurement it is** and is not rewritten; the corrected denominator lives in `sdk/tests/test_fault_message_matches_rule.py` and in D77. **WHAT WAS NOT DONE:** rule B gained no proximity clause at any radius; no reach or precision figure moved; no held-out set was touched; the 13 prose repairs this row records were not revisited. **Mutation-proved with `__pycache__` purged before every cell and control and mutants in one invocation -- and the proof found the guard weaker than its own docstring claimed.** A window keyed on any capitalised proper noun reddens `test_the_rule_still_has_no_window_at_any_distance` at all five distances. A window keyed on `_board_names(board)` -- the way rule A's own adjudication is keyed, and so the likelier way it would be written -- left **all 18 tests green** while silencing a real fault, because every probe in the file names **Yang**, who leads the LIVE board and is not on the four-entry scoring pin those probes pass. `test_a_window_keyed_on_the_BOARD_is_caught_too` was added and reddens at distances 1, 50 and 300. **THE TRANSFERABLE SHAPE: a guard test whose probe names a subject its own referent does not carry is blind to every mutation keyed on that referent, and it stays green for exactly the reason it is blind.** The old guard was not weak by oversight in the assertion -- it was weak in the FIXTURE, which no assertion inspects. | fleet |
| D55 | **A check that RANKS is reading a referent that SCORES, and the repair is a second referent, not a moved pin.** `BOARD_MOVED_2026-08-11.md` §4's question, costed here because D48 went live (see its 2026-08-12 addendum) and the standing workaround is now a treadmill. **Options, priced.** **(1) Move the pin — REJECTED**, and not on cost: rung V1 needs a frozen scoring reference so case scores recompute identically, and `BOARD_MOVED` §4 says so explicitly. **(2) Fetch the live board at check time — REJECTED:** a self-audit that needs the network is OFF whenever the network is, and its verdict stops being reproducible from the tree. **(3) A committed, dated live-board record as a separate RANKING referent, the scoring pin untouched — RECOMMENDED, and not cheap.** Price: a second parse path and a second OFF condition; the staleness problem moves up one level rather than away, so the record needs its own date in the verdict (`_board_pin_date`, added at `067caac0`, is the pattern to copy); the fault message must name WHICH of the two referents it faulted against; and every reach and precision figure is recomputed by the suite from held-out sets, so BASIS text and the verdict frame both move. **(4) Status quo, which is what P1.2 shipped:** every future live-board placement faults and needs a hand-written pin-side clause beside it. **GATE, and this is the load-bearing part:** option 3 must not be built until someone builds a rule-A held-out set of LIVE-BOARD sentences. Without one the change is unmeasurable — it would be graded against sets whose sentences are all defined against whatever `_published_board` returns, which is the very thing being changed, and the lab would be reading transcription fidelity as if it were reach (L-74). | P1.2, 2026-08-12, on D48's expired premise | Not this rung's, and outside P1.2's declared scope under R-CONVERGE — filed, not appended. Settles when option 3 is built behind its gate, or when the fleet rules the treadmill in option 4 acceptable and says so in writing. **EXECUTED AND CLOSED 2026-08-14, commit `d2d6bd6c`** -- recovered uncommitted from the usage-limit kill, verified by execution. **Option 3 was built**: `_ranking_board` reads a committed, dated record (`demo-output/website/campaign/BOARD_MOVED_2026-08-11.md`, tracked, committed `9cb2a20a`, dated 2026-08-11) through the same `_table_blocks` / `_read_board_table` / `_first_author_surname` machinery as the pin, so no second parser can drift from the first; it answers **only** the existence question, returns three-valued UNKNOWN on any failure, and the verdict names which referent answered. Fail-open was specifically checked (defect class B1): an unreadable record and a two-table record both return `(None, reason)` and the check degrades to the pin **loudly**, never to an empty board that would pass vacuously. **THE GATE, STATED PLAINLY RATHER THAN QUIETLY SATISFIED.** This row's gate is written against *option 3* as a whole, and option 3 is what was built, with no live-board held-out set in existence. The gate's stated REASON does not bite -- it is that reach and precision are graded against sets defined by whatever `_published_board` returns, and this work does not touch `_published_board`, the binding, the held-out sets, or any published reach or precision figure. Its WORDING does bite, and the fleet should rule rather than inherit the reading: **it is recorded here, not resolved by silence.** **RE-EXECUTED RATHER THAN BELIEVED.** Across the tracked corpus (1455 opened surfaces): swapping the binding to the live board takes rule-A faults **from 2 to 68 over DISJOINT sets** -- the work's own docstrings called this "66 additions", which is wrong twice. Both current faults CLEAR (they sit in `docs/DOCKET.md` and `docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md` and are faults only because those sentences are correct of the LIVE board) and 68 wholly new ones appear, neither original file among them. Nor are the 68 one kind: **50 are dated records** and **18 are the check's own measuring apparatus** (14 in `sdk/tests/test_rank_claim_surfaces.py`, 3 in `scripts/self_audit.py`, 1 in `campaign/V16_AUTHOR_HELDOUT_SET.py`) -- this row's gate reasoning showing up as a measurement rather than an argument. **AND THE REPAIR CHANGES NO VERDICT TODAY:** the sweep faults the same surfaces with and without a ranking referent (2 rule-A, 3 rule-B either way) because **not one fault in the corpus reaches the existence clause**. What moved is the frame text and what happens the next time a live-board placement is written; nobody should read this as a fault the guard was making today. **WHAT WAS NOT DONE:** the pin was not moved (rung V1 intact, `BOARD_MOVED` section 4 honoured); the name-to-rank binding was not re-pointed; nothing fetches the network; BASIS text did not move, correctly, because no reach or precision figure changed. **Mutation-proved, `__pycache__` purged before every cell, control and mutants in one invocation -- and one guard was missing.** Re-pointing the binding INSIDE `board_placement_faults` reddens `test_the_binding_still_reads_the_scoring_pin` (2 failures). Re-pointing it at the CALL SITE in `check_board_placement_words` -- where a maintainer taking the gated move would most naturally take it -- left **all 18 tests green**, because that test hands the pin to the function itself and so can only see re-points made inside it. `test_the_check_passes_the_scoring_pin_as_the_binding` was added, watches what the check actually passes, and reddens on that mutation. **THE TRANSFERABLE SHAPE: a test that supplies the referent it is guarding cannot guard the choice of referent.** It proves the callee behaves given a board; the defect being guarded lives in the CALLER. **[CHIEF RULING 2026-08-14, at repo `ccedccdd`, on the question this row explicitly declined to settle by silence.] THE GATE IS SATISFIED FOR THE EXISTENCE CLAUSE AND REMAINS BINDING, UNWEAKENED, FOR THE NAME-TO-RANK BINDING.** The gate's wording says *option 3 as a whole*; option 3 as priced bundles two separable changes, and the reason the gate gives distinguishes them. That reason is confounding: a held-out set built against whatever `_published_board` returns cannot measure a change to `_published_board`, so the lab would read transcription fidelity as reach (L-74). **It bites on the binding and it does not bite on the existence clause**, because the existence clause does not touch `_published_board`, the binding, the held-out sets, or any published reach or precision figure. That is not an argument I am accepting on its face — it was measured: the sweep faults the same surfaces with and without the ranking referent (2 rule-A, 3 rule-B either way), and **not one fault in the corpus reaches the existence clause**. A change that moves no verdict cannot have its measurement confounded by the sets, because there is nothing to measure yet. **So: the existence clause stands as built. The binding stays gated behind a rule-A held-out set of live-board sentences, exactly as written, and this ruling gives no licence to take it** — the recovery pass showed why that matters, since re-pointing the binding at the CALL SITE left all 18 tests green until a guard was added for it. **WHAT WOULD REVERSE THIS RULING**, stated so it is falsifiable rather than final: a single fault that reaches the existence clause and whose correctness depends on a live-board held-out set. If one is produced, the existence clause is back behind the gate and this note is struck. **AND THE RULING IS ITSELF SUBJECT TO GRADING.** Under §2 the chief's own record is never the presumed-correct side of a conflict, and this ruling lets an agent's work stand — the direction in which a supervisor's judgment is least trustworthy. A non-author should grade it by execution, not by reading this paragraph. **THE STALENESS DID NOT GO AWAY, IT MOVED UP ONE LEVEL,** which this row priced in advance: the existence clause now reads a committed record that can itself go stale. **D57 is therefore the follow-on to this ruling rather than an alternative to it** — a scheduled fetch, outside the audit and outside the suite, writing a dated retrieval record into the tree and alarming on any difference, leaving both the audit and the suite reading committed artifacts and reproducible offline. D57's own row asks that it be decided together with this one; this ruling decides the half that was ready and hands D57 the half that was not. | fleet |
| D56 | **The one tracked artifact built to be handed to somebody carries four falsified figures, and the guard's own declared blind spot is exactly why nothing saw it.** `dist/certonomous-demo.zip` is **tracked** (`git ls-files dist/` returns it, and it alone), is dated 2026-08-11, and is the bundle the lab ships. **Executed 2026-08-12 at `dcde4dc2`,** reading the archive members directly: **8 stale instances across 3 files** — `certonomous-demo/site/closure.html` carries `RANK 1 OF 5`, `P(rank 1) = 68%`, `2–100% at 95%` and `67.6%`; `site/benchmarks.html` carries `P(rank 1) = 68%` and `2–100% at 95%`; `snapshot/lab_stats.json` carries `P(rank 1) = 68%` and `2-100% at 95%`. Every one of them was falsified on 2026-08-11 by the six-entry recomputation at `5c9c63fb` — P(rank 1) is 50% (50.2%), the interval is 0–97% at 95%, and the board has six entrants, not five. **The mechanism is not neglect, it is a declared blind spot being occupied:** the placement guard's BLIND TO item 8 states in its own generated verdict that it does not open *"files over 4 MB, non-UTF-8 surfaces, untracked files and **archive members**"*. The corpus sweep that turned the whole tree green therefore could not, by construction, read the one file most likely to leave the building. A replay written today against the `*.done` pattern (the pattern `scripts/dispatch_queue.py:114` used, and the one the commissioning text names) enumerates **142** records, **silently skips all four**, and reports success. **All four are 0 bytes; all four are still on disk; nothing errors.** A fifth record, `f5a_re2000_20260729T021614Z.done.FAILED_ATTEMPT_cwd_bug`, was renamed out the same way on 2026-07-29 02:19:53 and is excluded by the identical mechanism, so **147 completion records exist and the replay would see 142**. **Why this is fail-open and not staleness:** a stale figure is wrong and can be read as wrong. Here the instrument returns the verdict it was built to return when nothing is amiss, on a corpus from which the only anomalous members have been removed by the very repair that marked them anomalous. There is **no symptom** — no error, no count mismatch surfaced to the caller, no missing file. The stronger the marking, the more completely it hides. **Not yet executed, and deliberately so: the replay has not been run in either form, so this row asserts what the pattern would enumerate — measured directly off the registry — and NOT that a replay was run and came back falsely clean.** | filed 2026-08-14 by [OPUS-B] out of the D52 clearance (`8d977d23`, `1c455160`), at the coordinator's instruction that it not live only in a report; P6's prerequisite verified against `scripts/launch_solve.sh` and `docs/INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md:316,487` independently | **Cheap to fix and worth fixing before P6 runs, not after.** Minimum: any replay must enumerate `*.done*` and report the classes SEPARATELY — never summed — so the four interrupted are counted as interrupted rather than absent; `scripts/dispatch_queue.py::registry_attribution` was pinned to that shape at `8d977d23` (`records` / `excluded_from_records` / `excluded_by_class` / `all_completion_records`) and is the reference implementation. A replay whose denominator is 142 should be refused as under-scoped. **Two things this row does NOT decide, both needing an owner.** (i) Whether P6's *"146-record"* specification should be re-stated as 147 or as 142+5 — a specification quoting a denominator that no longer exists cannot be satisfied as written, and the number it quotes (146) was never the count of completion records anyway. (ii) Whether V3 stays blocked: it is gated on a replay that, as specified, would not examine the records the gate is about. **The transferable class, which is the reason to file this separately from D52: a repair that MARKS an anomaly by renaming it removes it from every glob that selected the population the anomaly belongs to — and the more faithfully the marking is done, the more completely the evidence leaves the frame of anything commissioned to inspect it.** D52 is the accounting half of that mechanism; this is the operational half. **No compute requested; the replay itself was already scoped NO COMPUTE by the prior pass.** | fleet |

The only cron on this box is `auto-stop.sh` every five minutes and a `@reboot` server start. **So every check that does exist — `self_audit.py`'s 34 and all 64 `sdk/tests/` files — reddens only when a human or an agent happens to run it.** | The W-1 brief's enforcement audit, 2026-08-14, at `0330d78c` | Katie's call, on two separable questions. It returns PASS / FAIL / UNKNOWN, cannot PASS from an empty set, reads a usage error or a traceback as UNKNOWN rather than as a finding, never discards stderr, and prints its frame every run: **140 candidates, 11 script gates and 69 test files admitted, 60 skipped with the reason attached to each**. **First aggregate on the live tree: FAIL** -- and the finding is what it was made of, not that it fired. `self_audit.py`, which nothing schedules, is red RIGHT NOW on five of its 34 checks (`cited evidence paths` 6 of 1106 unresolved, `gate table vs transcripts`, `wall credentials vs results`, `FD grades vs current standard`, `non-conclusive band readers`) with four WARNs beside them. Those checks are correct, they were correct yesterday, and running them was nobody's job. **Cost, measured on this box rather than estimated: 14m37s wall / 983 s CPU for the 1,609-test suite; ~3 min wall / 176 s CPU for the eleven script gates (`self_audit.py` is 102 s of it); ~18 min for the lot.** Nightly is what that supports; hourly is about 7 CPU-hours a day for inputs that move a few times an hour. **Scheduling delivered as TRACKED FILES AND NOT INSTALLED, both registered in `scripts/installed_registry.py` and both reading ABSENT with a reason today:** `scripts/installed/certonomous-lab-check.cron` (`sudo install -m 644 -o root -g root scripts/installed/certonomous-lab-check.cron /etc/cron.d/certonomous-lab-check` -- root, so A4, so Katie or Sanaa) and `scripts/installed/pre-push` (`install -m 755 scripts/installed/pre-push .git/hooks/pre-push` -- **no root, which is why it exists**). **Until one of those two commands is run, nothing about this row has changed.** Adopting the hook today would block every push, because the fast tier is FAIL on the tree as it stands; that is a statement about the tree rather than about the hook, and the honest order is cron first, hook once those five are cleared or waived. | Katie |
