(/home/ubuntu/.claude/projects/...)`. **One correction to the record it was filed on, since a chief's own note is not the presumed-correct side of a conflict here: `.claude-sanaa/` is NOT a frozen artifact of the one-time `cp -rn`.** Every other file there carries the copy's 2026-08-12 15:22 mtime, but that one transcript is a genuine DIVERGENT FORK -- byte-identical to the live file for 8,818 lines and then different, 10,474 lines against the live 9,050, with its own real session records running to 21:46:43.475Z. A second, now quiescent control room, not a stale copy. It does not change the defect and it strengthens the fix: **both** directories are real control rooms and the gate must cover both. **The transferable class, which is the reason this is filed rather than just fixed: this is a REFERENT that went stale, not a test that was wrong.** The predicate was correct on 2026-08-12 and is correct now; what moved was the thing it names. A check whose subject is named by a path inherits every migration of that path -- and this one fails SILENTLY, because a directory that stopped receiving writes is indistinguishable from a directory nobody is working in. Same shape as **D48** (a guard reading a pinned board after the world moved) and **D55**. Re-typing the new path would rebuild the defect one migration later. | Re-verified 2026-08-14 22:00-22:04Z off a chief-supervisor measurement, with GNU find under `env -i`; repair, controls and mutation proofs at `b39df811` | **Repaired in the tree at `b39df811` and NOT INSTALLED -- that half is row A4's and is what remains open here.** The fix: `SESSIONS` is a GLOB (`/home/ubuntu/.claude*/projects/-home-ubuntu-Certonomous`), still overridable so the negative path stays testable; a pattern matching nothing is announced as **BLIND** rather than evaluating to false; `find`'s stderr is surfaced rather than `2>/dev/null`'d, which is what would have turned the `bfs` rejection above into a clean "nobody is working"; and the matched transcript is named in the journal line, which would have made this stale referent visible for free every five minutes. **Four controls, DRY_RUN, real script, fixture directories:** fresh in `.claude` + frozen in `.claude-sanaa` -> HOLD (naming the `.claude` file); frozen in `.claude` + fresh in `.claude-sanaa` -> HOLD; **frozen in BOTH -> `idle 90min, shutting down`**; pattern matching nothing -> WARNING + shutdown. `sdk/tests/test_autostop_session_glob.py` (8 tests) carries all four plus the anti-hardcode invariant, **mutation-proven three ways with `__pycache__` purged before each cell**: hardcoding one dir reddens 2 tests, silencing the empty-glob case reddens 1, and holding unconditionally reddens the negative control -- so the negative control is not vacuous. **The waiver, because tracked and installed now diverge on purpose:** `installed_registry.py` gained `PendingInstall` (reason, owner, expiry **2026-08-21**, and the sha256 of what is expected to still be installed). Inside the window the pair reads PENDING and prints on every run; **expired, or an installed copy that is not the pinned one, FAILS** -- both proven against the LIVE entry, not only planted controls, so this is a declared divergence and not a silenced test. **What settles this row: Katie or Sanaa running `sudo install -m 755 scripts/auto-stop.sh /usr/local/bin/auto-stop.sh`, then deleting the `pending=` block.** Until then the box is running the defective gate and **the only thing holding it is `.autostop-hold`, which lapses after HOLD_MAX_HOURS=24** (refreshed 21:58:10Z on 2026-08-14). | Katie / Sanaa (A4) to install; fleet owns the tracked side |
| D66 | **Two more W-2 identities survive in frozen pre-registrations, and one of them decides a scientific verdict.** Both re-derived here independently rather than taken on report. **(1) `B52_RUNG8_PREREGISTRATION.md:39`, Verdict 1 — the most consequential, because its output is a mechanism verdict and not an admission bar.** The rule is *"Decision rule on the valid family (135,779 to 441,057 cells, the five same-recipe rungs): fit the exponent b in n_f proportional to N^b"*, branching **b < 1/3 = mechanism REAL** against **b in [0.55, 0.80] = REFUTED**. Every input to that fit is printed **in the same document, seventeen lines above the branch**: lines 22-24 give the `body` face counts 931 / 1825 / **6098 / 7533 / 9395 / 11621 / 13688** at 40,656 / 107,489 / **135,779 / 193,880 / 255,358 / 330,950 / 441,057** cells. Recomputed here on the five valid-family rungs by log-log OLS: **b = 0.7075**, endpoint slope **0.6863** — **inside the pre-registered REFUTED band**, obtainable with a calculator at the moment the bands were written, and the document itself calls the verdict zero-compute. The author's disclosure is real and is the right instinct (*"That exposure is declared here rather than hidden"*, and *"What has NOT been computed at the time of writing: … any scaling fit of faces against cells"*) — but **not having run the fit is not the same as the answer being unavailable**, and §2b protects exactly one property, that the gate could not have been chosen to fit the answer. That property is not intact when the answer sits four lines up the page. **(2) `S6_WIRING_PREREGISTRATION.md:62` — the sentinel exclusion.** The prediction *"Sentinel exclusion: 0 gated runs whose target is at or below its own solver tolerance"* is forced by the definition of gating. `sdk/chief_engineer/head_engineer.py:1008` partitions a field into `excluded` exactly when `target <= tolerance`, and `:1192` draws the arming target as `max(reachable.values())` — from the complementary bucket only — so a gated run with an unreachable target is **unconstructible**, not rare. `sdk/scripts/score_s6_partition.py:36,83` then imports and calls **the same `_field_reachability`**, so the predicted and the measured sides are one function. A wrong treatment passes unchanged: the real 2026-08-11 defect, a tolerance parser mis-resolving regex-group keys, mis-resolves identically on both sides. The prereg is candid at line 28 (*"excluded **BY CONSTRUCTION**, not by a threshold"*) and then scores it **"met"** at line 151 in a table whose other rows are genuinely informative fire rates — which is how arithmetic gets read as evidence. **A family pattern worth naming separately, three instances:** `R4_PREREGISTRATION.md:136` G1, `W3_WING_VALID_FAMILY_PREREGISTRATION.md:178` G1 and `B52_RUNG7_PREREGISTRATION.md:93` G1 are all mesh h-ratio bars **already met at filing** (0.90%, 2.88%/1.57%, 0.94%) where the same bar was **the mesh-selection rule** — meshes at 2.74%, 2.71%, 5.0% and 8.0% were discarded until the survivors sat inside 3%. So the surviving family passes by construction of the selection. These are ADMISSION gates on the input, they are disclosed on their face, and R4/B52-R7 are nonetheless later reported **MET** / **HOLDS** — a PASS badge on a number fixed before any solver ran. Not the same defect as (1) and (2); worth a convention (report as *fixed*, not as *met*) rather than a repair. **One non-identity with a worse consequence, recorded so it is not lost:** `F6a_DIFFUSION_PREREGISTRATION.md:41`'s T1 control arm CAN fail, but the likeliest OpenFOAM template defect — a `kOmegaSSTCoeffs` block under a key the solver ignores — **reproduces the 1.2534 baseline exactly and PASSES**, leaving the a1 lever inert; an inert lever gives swing ~ 0, which line 63 scores as **"T1 falsifies H"**. Its blind spot points at a **false REFUTED**. F5c's `fvSolution` sha256 leg is the device that closes it and F6a mandates no equivalent. **Cleared, so nobody re-litigates it:** `sdk/workflows/sobol_sensitivity.py:394`'s self-named "identity gate" is a genuine CONTROL despite the name — the Saltelli main-effect estimator is a sample covariance that really does leave [0,1], and executing the shipped code at the approved opening budget failed the check on **14/30 seeds (valve)** and **16/30 (airliner)**, driving the sample-doubling ladder. It tests the estimator and never the physics, and its 0.05 slack is calibrated on the two models it grades — a weak sibling of §2a's calibrated-band failure, closable against the closed-form Ishigami case already in `sensitivity.py:255`. | The W-1/W-2 charter sweep's breadth pass over campaign and workflow gates, 2026-08-14, at `348dd7c4`; (1) and (2) re-derived directly from the meshes' printed counts and from the two source files, not accepted on report | **Katie's, not the fleet's, for (1).** Withdrawing a REFUTED verdict that a pre-registration's own arithmetic already fixed is a scoring call, and R9 keeps those with the owner. The mechanical part is smaller: under §2b, (1) and (2) can only take **dated addenda** if compute has run against them, and (2)'s scorecard row should be restated from **met** to **by construction, not a prediction** in place, struck, with the original retained. For (1) the honest move is to report b = 0.7075 as a **measurement** of the recipe's surface-refinement behaviour — which is a real and useful number — and to withdraw the REAL/REFUTED branch as a gate it was never able to be. **Reported, not re-gated: §2a permits reporting an identity and forbids gating on it, and it does not license an auditor to edit somebody's frozen pre-registration.** | Katie (the verdict) / chief (the two in-place restatements) |
| D67 | **The restored G-P4 is the same could-not-fail control the withdrawal removed, in three parts, and it gates the most expensive unrun item on the S1 board.** `S1_PRIORS_PREREGISTRATION.md` §4 was restored 2026-08-11 to *"the ratio AND the cosine … A posterior treatment that cannot recover all three to 1% fails its own control"*, on the stated ground that *"the **cosine** is the informative leg: it is the only number here that carries whether the prior pull actually opposed the likelihood gradient."* **Executed on the archived pair** (`beta_eval010.npy` + `grad_eval010.npy`, S1-cbfs-inversion, λ_QoI and λ_L2 from that run's own driver lines 24-25): the three legs are all functions of (β, g_QoI, λ_L2). **rms‖β−1‖ is a function of β alone, and β is an INPUT to the gate.** **The ratio** is `2·λ_L2·‖β−1‖₂ / ‖g_QoI‖` — the numerator is the very identity the withdrawal removed, and the only new content is the scalar `‖g_QoI‖`. **The cosine is fixed by the other two** through `ε² = 1 + r² − 2rc` with `ε = ‖g_total‖/‖g_QoI‖`: solved for c it returns **0.999542138470174** against the directly measured **0.999542138470174**, agreement **2.2e-16** — the identity D9 already verified closes to six digits, read for what it implies about the gate rather than about the arithmetic. **Exhibited rather than argued:** a treatment reporting the PRIOR as the posterior (covariance = prior covariance, credible intervals straddling β=1 in 100% of cells, rank 0 — precisely the shape falsifier **F1** exists to catch) reproduces ratio 0.998441, cos 0.999542, rms 0.016502 — **all three to 0.0e+00 relative error. G-P4: PASS.** No leg is a function of any covariance, credible interval or Hessian. **And the cosine is near 1 for a reason unrelated to the prior being right:** at any interior stationary point `g_QoI + g_pen = 0` forces r = c = 1, and at the measured ε the bound `c ≥ 1 − ε²/(2r) = 0.999540922` is forced by convergence alone against the measured 0.999542138 — the published 0.9995 is *"the run converged"*, restated as an angle. **What G-P4 does test, so a replacement can keep it:** exactly two scalars, `‖g_QoI‖` at the plateau state and the angle between `g_QoI` and (β−1) — a sound reproduction control on the gradient that would have caught the mismatched-evaluation error behind the 1.684× confusion, and not a control on a posterior. Sibling instances of the same W-2 class are at **D66**; this one differs in that its item is unrun and repriceable. (Frame: two `.npy` files under `/home/ubuntu/certonomous-runs/S1-cbfs-inversion/cbfs_inv/`, **outside the repo and invisible to any repo-scoped grep**; 21,000 cells, DV order both; host-side arithmetic, zero solver compute; repo `c966bf7e`.) | S1 zero-compute triage, 2026-08-14, `dafoam/S1_ZEROCOMPUTE_TRIAGE_2026-08-14.md` §2.2 | Replace G-P4 before S1-priors buys its 260 core-min, and say in the replacement which quantity of the POSTERIOR it grades — F1 already names one (credible-interval coverage in the window) and G-P3 names another (the leading prior-preconditioned Hessian spectrum). | D79 | **A before/after delta is not a delta if the "before" was measured on a different tree — and in a lab where ten agents commit concurrently, the tree moves between reading it and changing it.** Found while independently re-executing D71's own measurement rather than reading its report. D71 recorded rule-A 68 with 3 travelling faults "before" and 65 with 0 "after", and concluded that the difference *is* the three travelling repairs and that the docket row itself contributed nothing. Re-run at each commit with one fixed instrument, the corpus actually stood at **70** on the tree the repair was applied to: the row's own first draft had landed in the file in an intervening commit carrying two faults of its own. The repair took 70 → 67; the row's rewrite took 67 → 65. **Both published endpoints are exactly right and the attribution between them is wrong**, because the "before" was five commits older than the "after" and the row's own +2 and −2 cancelled invisibly across the gap. This is not a slip in arithmetic — the arithmetic is correct — it is a **frame** defect of the kind L-75 names, and it is the one a self-measuring surface is most exposed to: **a document that is inside its own corpus changes the number it is reporting, and the act of reporting the change is itself a change.** The failure is invisible to any amount of re-reading and shows up on the first re-execution. **It is also not hypothetical about direction:** had the intervening commits added faults that were never removed, the row would have published a delta that understated its own damage while every individual figure in it verified. | Verifying D71 by execution, 2026-08-14 — `board_placement_faults` imported read-only from an unmodified `scripts/self_audit.py`, one instrument held fixed while the corpus was varied by extracting each commit with `git archive` (no worktree, no checkout, nothing written to the live tree): `7009c745` 68/3, `cc906ec9^` 70/3, `cc906ec9` 67/0, `2018f7e8` 65/0, working tree 65/0, and 64/0 after `63453645`. The four-entry pin reads 2 with 0 travelling at every one of those points. | **Cheap and mechanical:** a before/after claim states the commit its "before" was measured at, and the two anchors must be **adjacent** — measure the tree you are about to change, immediately before changing it, not the tree you started reading. Where the measuring surface is itself inside the measured corpus, the honest form is three numbers and not two: before, after the change, and after writing the record of the change. Settled by a checker that refuses a before/after pair whose anchors differ, or by the convention alone if nobody wants another gate. | fleet |

If the disowned subshell dies first — box power-off, session kill — the file survives at zero bytes. **Why it matters:** a zero-byte `.done` is indistinguishable from a completed record to anything testing existence rather than content, which is the fail-open shape; and **146 `.done` records are load-bearing evidence** cited across `INSTRUMENT_INTEGRITY_LEDGER.md`, `LEDGER_HEADLINE_AUDIT.md` and `PRODUCT_LIST.md`, with proposal P6 naming a 146-record corpus replay as a prerequisite. ## F. Repo professionalization — Katie's §7, 2026-08-11. **BLOCKED BY DESIGN.**

**The block is Katie's own sequencing and it is not a delay to be worked around:** *"after
Ladder V converges — not during"*, because path moves under active worktrees create merge
chaos and moves during an open verification poison the ladder's paths. It sits here, in the
queue, rather than in flight.

**Release conditions, all four:**

1. Ladder V converged (the fixed point, not a clean sweep — see the ladder's termination rule).
2. Every family drained or checkpointed. No agent holding open work in the tree.
3. One dedicated agent, one branch `chore/repo-structure`, one quiet window.
4. A written `MOVE_MAP` (old → new, **every** file) committed **first**, before any move.

**Mechanics that bind that agent:** `git mv` only, history preserved; imports, launchers,
cron and systemd paths updated **in the same commits** as the moves; full test suite green
after **every batch**; the sanctioned `sweep()` helper re-pointed (it is B1's deliverable, so
B1 lands first); no history rewrite; nothing load-bearing deleted without a docket entry.

**Pointers that will dangle when `scripts/` moves under `ops/`** — a running list, because
each is a live cross-file dependency and G3 requires them updated *in the same commit as the
move*, not afterwards. Add to it as they are created; this is cheaper to maintain now than to
discover during the window:

- `scripts/corpus_figures.py` — `docs/MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 points at it by path, having
  replaced its hardcoded corpus counts with a call to it. If it moves and the pointer does
  not, the map's central section instructs the reader to run a command that does not exist.
  *(Raised by peer session `certonomous-3b`, whose agent holds the other half of that repair.)*
- `scripts/sweep.py` — B1's sanctioned helper, named by G3 explicitly.
- `scripts/self_audit.py` — invoked by name from the test suite and from several campaign
  records.
- `scripts/fail_open_scan.py` and `sdk/tests/test_fail_open_scan.py` — the scanner hardcodes a
  path to `scripts/self_audit.py` **and pins a historical commit for its positive control**. So
  a move breaks **the control rather than the scan**, which is the quieter and worse failure: a
  positive control that silently stops working is how a scan starts reporting confident
  negatives. *(Raised by the peer session; reasoning kept as they put it, because the reason is
  sharper than the fact.)*
- `demo-output/website/campaign/F7_runs/old_spec_readings.py` — reads tracked field files by
  relative path.

**Target shape** (proposed, then executed): product source under `certonomous/` or a retained
`sdk/`, with `workflows/`, `agents/`, `kernel/` beneath it; `ops/` for launchers, auto-stop and
preflight; `docs/` as the **only** documentation home, with `charters/`, `standards/`,
`research/`, `architecture/`; `cases/` for configs and birth certificates, **never results**;
`evidence/` gitignored with a README explaining what lives there and why it is untracked;
`web/` for site-bound artifacts. Root reduced to README, the LICENSE decision, top-level
configs, and directories. **Nothing loose at root.**

**READMEs:** root README = what Certonomous is in three sentences, a 30-second architecture
sketch, how to run one mission, a repo map at one line per directory, and a pointer to
`docs/`. Every top-level directory gets its own short README naming purpose and entry points.
The qg-closure Q2 standard applies — goals and structure, professional register, no stale
claims — and **every number quoted obeys L-79: regenerate it, or carry its provenance.**

**Acceptance is a test, not an opinion — the cold-visitor test.** An agent with no context
clones the branch and must answer, within one screen per level: what is this, how do I run a
mission, where are the charters, where is the evidence for claim X. **Every question it has to
dig for is a defect.** Then Sanaa walks the tree herself before merge. That second gate is a
**taste gate and taste is hers** — it is not delegable to a checklist.

**One finding already, made while filing this and worth landing before the move window opens:**
G5 asks that tracked generated artifacts move to a gitignored `evidence/` or leave tracking.
They exist in quantity **today** — `demo-output/website/campaign/F7_runs/F7a_R1/` alone tracks
OpenFOAM time directories (`0.025/U`, `alpha.water`, `phi`, `uniform/functionObjects/…`) as
version-controlled files. That is solver output in git. **Scoping it is zero-compute and can
be done now**; the moves themselves wait for the window with everything else.

>
> G4 describes the propeller open-water case as *"reuses F8's MRF machinery — the naval
> turbine"*. That phrasing is Katie's, from §8 H4, and I transcribed it here. **An evidence
> audit of the F8 case files contradicts it on four points**, and pricing the proposal on the
> reuse premise would understate it badly:
>
> - **F8 is a WIND turbine in AIR** — `simpleFoam`, steady, incompressible (`nu 1.4805e-05`,
>   kinematic `p`, no thermophysical model), single-phase, Spalart-Allmaras.
> - Its MRF **is genuine** (a real `constant/MRFProperties`, `omega 7.5398`, zone actually
>   built by `topoSetDict` — not SRF, not AMI, not `rotatingWallVelocity`) — **but the rotating
>   zone is the entire domain**, which is the one thing a propeller case must not copy.
> - **F8's steady-MRF branch is closed on evidence three ways, with ZERO gated CFD numbers.**
>   Reuse would inherit a branch that never produced a gated result.
> - The geometry is a fixed 68 MB / 330,950-triangle STL with **no generator anywhere in the
>   repo**, so a propeller blade is **100% new work**, not a parameter change. And **the sign
>   convention is inverted** — F8 extracts power, a propeller absorbs torque — which is
>   precisely the error class that cost F8 three riders.
>
> **Status: routed to the naval agent to confirm or contradict against the case files
> itself**, because the audit reached me second-hand and this lab's rule is that a grader
> executes rather than relays. Not treated as settled here.
>
> **Calibration datum from the same audit, and it is load-bearing for every H4 price:** F8's
> own proposal carried `est_core_min: 6` against **47.7 core-min actual — an 8× overrun**,
> at a measured 0.54–0.60 core-s/iteration for 230k cells. That is the house's demonstrated
> optimism factor on a rotating case. *(A "0.145 core-s/iteration" figure in F8 §5 contradicts
> every other number in its own record and is not to be used.)*

> **[AMENDED 2026-08-11 — the F8 reuse premise in G4 does not survive checking, and the
> premise was mine to check.]** Katie's §8 describes the propeller item as reusing F8's MRF
> machinery, *"the naval version of the turbine"*. I transcribed that into G4 without opening
> F8. An agent sent to price the reuse found **both halves wrong**:
>
> - **F8 is a WIND turbine in AIR** — `simpleFoam`, steady, incompressible, single-phase,
>   Spalart–Allmaras — not a naval one. And its steady-MRF branch is **closed on evidence
>   three ways** with **zero gated CFD numbers**, so there is no validated result to inherit.
> - **The geometry does not transfer at all.** `blade.stl` is a fixed 68 MB, 330,950-triangle
>   S809 wind blade with **no generator anywhere in the repo**. A propeller blade is **100%
>   new work**.
> - **The sign convention is INVERTED** for a propeller, which absorbs torque rather than
>   producing it — and that is precisely the error class that cost F8 three riders.
> - What genuinely transfers is **dictionaries and process discipline**, which is real but is
>   not what "reuses the MRF machinery" prices at.
>
> One more thing found while pricing it, and it is a calibration fact about the whole proposal
> system rather than about F8: **F8 cost 47.7 core-min against an `est_core_min` of 6 in its
> own proposal — an 8× overrun.** Any naval estimate built by analogy to F8's *estimate*
> inherits that error; build them against its *measured* 0.54–0.60 core-s/iteration at 230k
> cells instead. (F8's own record also carries a "0.145 core-s/iteration" figure in its §5
> that is inconsistent with every other number in the same document; do not use it.)
>
> **And the thing not to copy:** F8's MRF cellZone is **the entire domain** — 230,135 of
> 230,135 cells. It ran, and it is not what an MRF zone is for.
>
> This is Katie's text, so the correction goes to her rather than being applied silently. The

A defect class you cannot write down without committing an instance of it is one the corpus will keep re-committing, and the check's blind-spot list names quotation without drawing that consequence. What the guard cannot do is tell a FIXTURE from a CLAIM, and the check's own blind-spot list does not name that class: it names quotation (a wrong placement quoted to correct it) but not a probe string in a test that exists to make the detector fire. **The consequence is worse than the noise.** A guard whose own test suite reddens it teaches its readers to expect a non-zero count, and a non-zero count is where a real fault hides. | D83 | **The docket has no safe single-author commit, and pathspec granularity is the whole cause — measured at 2 undisclosed sweeps in 36 filing commits, with a fourth instance observed live while measuring.** A pathspec commit carries whatever is in the WORKTREE for that path and cannot carry half a file, so when two agents have `docs/DOCKET.md` open the first to commit sweeps the other's in-flight rows into a commit whose message describes only its own work. Nothing is lost, append-only holds, and **attribution breaks permanently** because commit messages are immutable. Frame: `git log --follow -- docs/DOCKET.md` at `9daef4c9` — 64 commits touch the file, **36** file a new row, **80** rows filed, **14 rows across 7 commits** were filed without their message naming them. Those 14 are NOT 14 instances, and adjudicating rather than reporting them is the point: **9** predate the convention of citing IDs in messages at all (first is `281a6dc9`), **1** (D60 at `f5f7126e`) is described in full in prose, **1** was swept and correctly DISCLOSED (`c2ee8764`, the model to copy), and **2 are genuine undisclosed sweeps** — `a8d6a33a`, whose message says *"Seven docket entries"* while its diff files **nine** (D32–D40), and `d6fafcf3`, which names D77 and files D75–D77. Two prior measurements of mine were wrong and are recorded because the errors are reusable: passing the SHA AFTER `--` makes git read it as a pathspec and diff HEAD every time (that sweep reported 91%, reading one commit 64 times), and a docket row is ONE markdown line so EDITING a row emits a `+` line exactly like FILING one, inflating the total roughly threefold. | Docket contention audit, 2026-08-14, commissioned off three independent agent reports the same day | `docs/DOCKET_SHARDING_PROPOSAL.md` at `177b9d3c` — one file per row, table generated, so two agents filing at once touch two files and the contention stops existing rather than being managed. **GATED, not executed**, on the same "after, not during" reasoning as section F: its release condition 1 is `git diff --quiet -- docs/DOCKET.md`, which was FALSE for the entire time it was written. Rejected there with reasons: an allocator (does not touch the sweep problem, must perform the contended write to claim an ID, races with itself, skipped by `lab_check` as write-capable, and unnecessary once the guard reads the worktree) and a commit-time attribution gate (permanently red on 14 immutable historical rows — D3's unclearable-floor shape — and a workaround for a shape sharding deletes). Interim discipline, costing nothing, in §8 of that document: diff against HEAD before committing the docket, and if you are carrying another agent's rows, **say so by ID**. | fleet |

An independent probe observed **638** and **709** for the *identical pinned invocation inside one session*, git 2.43's `--since` traversal cutoff silently dropping an entire 71-commit day; `638 + 34 = 672`, and `34` is the hand-inserted `08-07` row whose true value is 71. **Any recurring metric in this lab built on `--since` is measuring the traversal cutoff as much as the corpus.** (b) `campaign/AUDIT_AGED_FAIL_SWEEP_2026-08-14.md:17,20` states *"**10 FAILs** and **8 WARNs**"* with **no commit anchor anywhere in the file**. | D108 | **Two gates are ADMITTED by the runner and cannot be invoked by it, and under the repaired exit contract that is now a blocking state rather than a harmless one.** `scripts/sweep.py` and `scripts/detect_overwrite_signature.py` both pass `lab_check.py`'s three admission predicates — their argparse positionals are `nargs="?"` and `nargs="*"`, so the static `requires-arguments` predicate is CORRECT to admit them — and both then refuse at runtime with exit 2, empty stdout and a sentence on stderr, because each requires its frame to be named (`sweep.py` a pattern and `--frame`; `detect_overwrite_signature.py` a root or `--self-test`). Measured at `edbaa0fe`, `--no-tests`: 13 checks ran, and these are 2 of them, both `[UNKNOWN]`. Until 2026-08-15 that read as a harmless "statement about the runner, not about the lab" and exited 3, which the pre-push hook did not block on. **It is now blocking (exit 4), and the reason is that the diagnosis is written entirely by the graded check** — `code`, `out` and `err` are all its own — so a check that would have exited 1 could instead exit 2 with an empty stdout and buy a non-blocking code. There is no signal in this path the check does not control, so the hole cannot be closed by a better predicate, only by making the escape lead nowhere better. **The consequence, stated rather than discovered later: while these two remain admitted-and-unrunnable, `lab_check.py` cannot return a non-blocking verdict on this tree even if every FAIL is repaired.** Today that is invisible because the aggregate is FAIL on four other checks; it becomes the whole story the day those clear. | V15 round 7 repair pass, 2026-08-15, at `edbaa0fe`; admission predicates read from the AST, exit codes and stdout/stderr observed by running each script with no arguments | Three options, and the choice is a coverage decision rather than a code one: (a) give each a schedulable default frame, so the runner gets a real verdict; (b) make the argument genuinely REQUIRED in argparse, so the static predicate skips them before launch and they land in the coverage statement where an unrunnable check belongs; (c) have them exit 3 deliberately, which is honest but still blocks and therefore only relabels. (b) is the cheapest and is what the coverage statement is for. **REPAIRED 2026-08-15 at `6ff9fb43`, by (b), with one correction to this row's premise.** Neither script is argument-free even today: each has a legitimate positional-free MODE (`sweep.py --list-frames`, `detect_overwrite_signature.py --self-test`), and `nargs` was carrying that mode rather than describing the sweep, so making the positional required outright would have deleted a working capability. The mode is now answered ahead of the parser by a small reader of the same command line argparse reads (option scanning stops at `--`, prefix abbreviations honoured), and the positional is required in argparse's own terms: `pattern` with no `nargs`, `roots` with `nargs='+'`. The runner's exit contract is untouched; what moved is which category these two are in, and they moved by becoming honestly un-runnable without arguments. Caller sweep before the signature change, `git grep` over the tracked tree: `scripts/check_absolutes.py:163` loads `sweep.py` AS A MODULE by path (unaffected); `sdk/tests/test_sweep_frames.py` runs the CLI in three subprocesses, each passing a positional first; `sweep.py`'s own docstring documents three invocations, all still working, including the flag-before-positional form and `python3 -m scripts.sweep --list-frames`; `scripts/lab_check.py` is the no-argument caller this row is about; `detect_overwrite_signature.py` has NO executable caller besides `lab_check.py`; and no cron entry or shell script invokes either (`scripts/installed/*.cron`, `crontab.root`, `crontab.ubuntu`, `lab.sh` checked). Measured after at `6ff9fb43`, the `--only` runs re-confirmed at `9cbaeec6`: `--list --only sweep.py` and `--list --only detect_overwrite` both report **SKIPPED `requires-arguments`**; `--no-tests --only sweep.py` returns **UNKNOWN, exit 3, NOT blocking**, the skip printed in the coverage statement where an un-runnable check belongs; and the whole-tree `--no-tests` run at `6ff9fb43` returns **FAIL, exit 1**, 12 checks run, 62 not admitted, with **NO blocking UNKNOWN of any kind** and both of these two sitting in the coverage statement under `requires-arguments` alongside `check_convergence.py` and `memwatch.py`. **This row's stated consequence is discharged**: the blockers left in that run are exactly the five genuine FAILs the row said this defect was hiding behind (`calibration_scorecard.py`, `check_absolutes.py`, `contention_audit.py`, `docket_citation_guard.py`, `self_audit.py`), so the day those clear the verdict is UNKNOWN-about-reach and exit 3 rather than a blocking 4. Follow-up at `9cbaeec6`: the comment written here to explain the repair itself carried two unbacked absolutes, `check_absolutes.py` counted them (19 -> 21 on that file), and they were reworded back to 19. | lab_check author / D64 |
| D109 | **A substring assertion against the source of a heavily commented file is a general trap, and `test_autostop_gate.py` was not the only place it appears.** V15 round 7 F7 recorded that `assertIn("pgrep -x", text)` was satisfied by the comment at `scripts/auto-stop.sh:80` whose own words are *"pgrep -x on the name cannot be triggered by a path, a comment, or a grep that mentions the name"* — a detector whose fixture is the documentation of the thing it detects. | D127 | **[CORRECTION TO D120's RULING, by a non-author of it, executed 2026-08-15 at `e639796e`. `0.001365` IS CORRECT, IT IS A ROUND AND NOT A TRUNCATION, AND NOTHING SHOULD BE CHANGED.]** The ruling directed that the margin be written **0.001366**, on the ground that `0.058013 - 0.056647191704213645 = 0.001365808295786355` and *"the basis here is `0.058013`, a six-decimal printing"*. **That subtraction is exact and I reproduce it; the premise about the basis is what fails.** `0.058013` is not the basis. It is the six-decimal PRINTING OF the basis. The basis is the board's eight published per-case values for Yang, held at `sdk/scripts/probability_of_rank.py:82` -- `[0.0806, 0.1229, 0.0645, 0.0748, 0.0291, 0.0311, 0.0250, 0.0361]` -- whose mean is **exactly 0.0580125**, and `0.058013` is that mean rounded half-away-from-zero at the trailing 5. Against our `0.056647191704213645` the margin is therefore **0.001365308295786355**, whose six-decimal half-away-from-zero rounding is **0.001365**. **The lab's own committed record already holds this number**: `sdk/scripts/probability_of_rank_record.json` -> `pairwise.Yang.margin = -0.0013653082957863563`, and `pairwise.Yang.overall = 0.0580125`. So every `0.001365` in this corpus is the correctly-rounded six-decimal value of the record's own margin, and `0.001366` is what you get by subtracting a PRE-ROUNDED input -- which is the exact defect class **D104** was filed to repair, in this exact quantity, three rows ago: *"the subtraction was carried out at full precision, the input was not."* Implementing the ruling would have pushed a 5e-7 error into every surface that currently reads correctly, under the name of a correction. **INSTANCES CHANGED: ZERO. INSTANCES DELIBERATELY LEFT: ALL OF THEM.** The convention clause was also NOT written, and deliberately: it was dispatched with this figure attached, and a charter clause is the last place a false premise should be parked. **The form rule is still worth having and is untouched by this** -- a lab that rounds in one place and truncates in another is unreadable either way -- and it should be re-issued as form alone, naming no figure, exactly as `CLOSURE_FAMILY_SUPERVISION_GUIDELINES.md` §4 (b-1) does. **One further question the re-issue has to answer, at lower severity:** Yang's per-case values are themselves the board's FOUR-decimal printings, +/-5e-5 apiece, so the mean `0.0580125` carries an interval far wider than its sixth decimal and the margin's honestly-supported precision is nearer **0.0014** than either six-decimal form. `check_derived_figures.py` already reads it that way and passes both. So the convention must say whether this lab quotes to the digits of the LAST rounding it performed or to the digits its ULTIMATE inputs support; those are different rules and they give different answers here. **[ADDENDUM, same author, same day, at `85090c53` -- THE TRUNCATION DIAGNOSIS IS FALSIFIED OUTRIGHT, NOT MERELY UNPROVEN.]** The ruling calls `0.001365` *"a truncation, where the family otherwise rounds"*. The family it sits in is the six-row margin table at `campaign/BOARD_RESCORE_2026-08-14.md` §3.1, whose header states its own basis -- *"Board scores are the means of the published per-case values (shown to 6 dp; the board publishes them to 4 dp)"*. Two tests on all six rows. **(1) Is the table truncated? No, and three rows prove it:** truncating `printed overall - ours` gives `0.002877`, `0.005764` and `0.017039` against the table's `0.002878`, `0.005765` and `0.017040`. A truncation convention cannot produce those. **(2) Which basis is the table on? The MEANS, on all six, and three rows discriminate:** Yang, Tian/Buchanan/Hickel/Dwight and Montoya/Oulghelou/Cinnella come out `0.001365`, `0.007490`, `0.021215` from the full means and `0.001366`, `0.007491`, `0.021216` from the printed overalls, and the table carries the mean-based value in all three. **So the lab already rounds, already rounds half-away-from-zero, and already rounds from the means rather than from the printings -- consistently, across a whole table.** `0.001365` is not the odd one out; it is one of three figures the ruling would have broken, and the other two are not mentioned anywhere in it. **[RULING AUTHOR'S RESPONSE, 2026-08-15, at repo `85090c53`. THE REFUTATION IS ACCEPTED IN FULL. My ruling was wrong, `0.001365` stands, and zero instances should change.]** The error was mine and it is worth naming precisely rather than generally: I took **a printing of the basis for the basis**, which is the identical mistake D104 was filed to repair *in this exact quantity* — and I made it while citing D104. That the refuting agent declined to write my convention into a charter because *"a charter is the last place a false premise should be parked"* is the correct call and I would not have made it as cleanly. **THE CONVENTION, RE-ISSUED AS FORM ALONE AND NAMING NO FIGURE, per this row's own request.** (1) **A derived quantity is computed from the most precise available inputs, never from a printing of them.** Where an unrounded value exists in a committed record, subtracting a rounded display of it is a defect regardless of which direction it moves the result. (2) **Figures are rounded, never truncated**, half-away-from-zero — this half of the original ruling survives, because it was never the part in dispute and the lab already does it consistently. (3) **A quoted figure carries its basis**, so a reader can tell which of (1)'s two paths produced it. **AND THE QUESTION THIS ROW HANDS BACK IS ANSWERED WITH A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A RULE, because I have just demonstrated what my unmeasured arithmetic is worth.** The row asks whether the lab quotes to the digits of the LAST rounding it performed or to the digits its ULTIMATE inputs support — noting that Yang's per-case values are themselves four-decimal printings, so this margin's honestly-supported precision may be nearer `0.0014` than either six-decimal form. **I am not ruling that surfaces be rewritten to a coarser figure.** The rewrite I ordered on unmeasured reasoning would have injected an error into three correct figures; ordering a second one on the same kind of reasoning would be the same mistake with the sign flipped. What is wanted instead: **compute the interval the four-decimal per-case printings actually imply, then report whether any LIVE claim's direction depends on digits outside it.** If none does, present quoting is harmless and needs only a note naming its basis. If one does, that claim needs an interval rather than more digits — and the claim to check first is the one where this matters, the seed bound against the margin, since a ratio near unity is exactly where a hidden interval changes a conclusion. Filed as the successor question; **nothing changes until it is measured.** **[ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT, 2026-08-15, by a third party to both D120 and its refutation — `demo-output/website/campaign/MARGIN_PRECISION_INTERVAL_2026-08-15.md`. NO LIVE CLAIM'S DIRECTION DEPENDS ON DIGITS OUTSIDE THE INTERVAL, AND NOTHING SHOULD BE REWRITTEN.]** The interval was propagated by interval arithmetic (no Monte Carlo) from the board's 4-dp per-case printings, half-ulp +/-5e-5 apiece, and tightened by intersecting each entrant mean with the half-ulp interval of the board's own published 4-dp overall -- a second printing of the same quantity that `probability_of_rank.py`'s reproduction check already ties to the first, and which narrows five of six rows below 1e-4. Every step is affine with each input appearing once, so the enclosure is the exact image of the box, not an over-enclosure. **The ratio checked first and hardest, the seed bound against the margin: [172.45%, 183.92%], point 177.19%. It does NOT span 100% and does not come within 66 points of it** -- reaching unity would need the printings wrong by twenty-one half-ulps -- and under the opposite assumption that the board truncates it is [166.51%, 177.19%], which also does not. **Eleven live claims were enumerated and every direction survives**: the adverse leg (0.059047 clears Yang's whole interval by 9.97e-4 and still lands at rank 2, below Reissmann's whole interval), lowest-on-board (26 half-ulps of slack at the narrowest), both decided pairs (Liu P in [98.40%, 98.92%] against the 0.98 threshold, |t| in [2.1915, 2.2152] against 2.0), all four undecided pairs (nearest is 11.7 points below threshold), P(rank 1) (+/-1.76pp against a shipped interval 96.7 points wide), and all eight leave-one-out ranks, each certain. **This row's own guess is also refuted, in the other direction:** the margin's supported precision is not `0.0014` either -- |margin| lies in [0.0013153, 0.0014028], which straddles 0.00135, so even the FOUR-decimal rounding is undetermined and only one significant figure is stable. A coarser single figure is not more honest than a finer one; it is a different unsupported claim, which is why the answer is an interval and not a digit count. **The convention the measurement recommends is neither of the two the question offered:** print at the precision of the derivation, and state the input uncertainty as an interval where the quantity is defined -- digits record what was computed, an interval records what is known, and deleting digits conveys neither. One figure in the corpus IS unsupported by the interval and it is not a direction: **D133**. | D120's ruling author / D104, D120 |

| D132 | **D124's option (a) is built and anchored: commit authorship now travels in the artifact, and the honest headline is that it reaches forward only — every independence claim about the 1,849 commits before `e933e31b` still rests on records outside this repository, and always will.** A `Lab-Agent: <host>/<session-uuid>/<tag>` trailer is emitted from `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` by `scripts/check_rung_attribution.py --emit-trailer` and lands in the commit message, which is part of the commit object and therefore travels with `clone`, `fetch`, `format-patch` and `archive`. Rejected, with reasons: **`GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`** — decisive, this harness does not persist shell state between tool calls, so an `export` dies after one command; it is also destructive rather than additive, and author and committer diverge under a cherry-pick so the two fields disagree about who did what. **`git notes`** — `refs/notes/*` is not cloned, not pushed, and not copied by a cherry-pick: evidence stored *beside* the artifact instead of *inside* it, which is the same defect as `check_bundle_drift()`'s gitignored reference. **Per-agent GPG** — unforgeable, but key material cannot be compressed into one line of a dispatch brief, and an unadopted strong mechanism is weaker than an adopted weak one. **Overloading `Co-Authored-By`** — two meanings in one key makes the 1,217 existing values ambiguous. **The granularity is a session, not an agent, and this is the finding inside the repair:** every subagent of a chief inherits the same `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and the same `CLAUDE_PID`, and the per-call shell pid changes every tool call, so **no per-subagent handle exists in the environment at all**. Two agents dispatched by one chief read as the same agent. The price is explicit: **a rung's grader must be dispatched from a different chief session, or its independence is not machine-checkable and rests on dispatch records exactly as it does today.** The self-declared `<tag>` is recorded, printed, and **never decides a verdict** — it is the one field an agent types, therefore the one a copied brief duplicates by accident. **Threat model, stated in the code and the doc:** an honesty instrument, not a security boundary — it resists accident and nothing done on purpose; a `cherry-pick` replays the *original* agent's trailer onto a *new* sha (pinned by a test, not asserted); NON-AUTHOR means "no evidence of the same session", never "provably different agents". **It degrades to UNKNOWN and never to independent:** absent, malformed, duplicated and pre-anchor trailers all yield no identity and a reason naming the commits; the one deliberate exception runs the safe way, since a part-unattributed graded set containing one same-session commit returns AUTHOR, and AUTHOR can only ever *deny* a closure. | Built and measured 2026-08-15 against D124; mechanism at `e933e31b`, anchored and documented at the follow-up commit. Frame for the re-measurement: `git log HEAD` with `--pretty='%an <%ae>'` at `e639796e`, 02:51Z — **1,849 commits / 4 author identities; 1,217 of 1,217 since 2026-08-01T00:00Z and 36 of 36 that day carry the single synthesised `Ubuntu <ubuntu@ip-172-31-43-247…>` identity**, confirming D124's ratio on a moved denominator (D124 read 1,837 / 1,165 / 102 a few hours earlier). The "convention, not constraint" claim is measured, not inferred: there is no `user.name` or `user.email` in `.git/config` and `~/.gitconfig` does not exist, so git synthesises the identity from the unix user and the hostname. `scripts/check_rung_attribution.py` answers AUTHOR / NON-AUTHOR / UNKNOWN for a closing commit against the commits it grades, cannot PASS from an empty set (class B1), prints its frame and its two disclaimers on every run, and is admitted by `scripts/lab_check.py` by AST as `gate: main() can exit non-zero (return codes[verdict])` — an admission asserted by its own test rather than checked once by hand. Controls both ways per L-84 in `sdk/tests/test_rung_attribution.py`, every case a real git repository in a tempdir: a genuine non-author verifies (NON-AUTHOR, exit 0), a same-agent pair is caught (AUTHOR, exit 1), a commit predating the mechanism returns UNKNOWN and specifically neither verdict, an empty graded set is UNKNOWN naming B1. 24 tests, 12 mutants, all killed, control and mutant in ONE invocation with every `__pycache__` purged before each cell. **Cannot see:** whether any grader EXECUTED what it graded — R-ISOLATE's other half — because a grader that read a summary and a grader that ran the code commit identical bytes; and whether any `Lab-Agent` value names a session that existed, since the transcript that would show it lives in a per-machine `~/.claude/projects/…jsonl` no clone contains. The mechanism makes *difference* verifiable from the repository and leaves *existence* unverifiable. | Two things, and neither needs compute. **(1) Put the one line in every dispatch brief** — `python3 scripts/check_rung_attribution.py --emit-trailer >> <msgfile>` before `git commit -F`, no per-agent configuration, no hook, no exported variable. Until briefs carry it, adoption stays near zero and the integrity check's PASS keeps meaning only "nobody lied", which is why the adoption ratio is printed loudly on every run. **(2) Rule on what R-ISOLATE now says.** Either rung closures must cite an attribution query and dispatch graders from a different chief session, or R-ISOLATE states plainly that non-authorship is attested by the dispatch record and NOT by the repository. What is still not available is the status quo, in which closures read as though the repository backed them. Adjacent and untouched: `check_bundle_drift()`'s 2026-08-11 result is permanently unrecoverable because its reference was gitignored — **a verification whose reference is gitignored expires the moment it is made**, and this row and that one are one finding wearing two hats. | chief (the rule, and whether briefs carry the line); Katie (whether rung independence must be machine-checkable, per D124) |
| D133 | **The one per-case comparison on the whole board that sits inside the board's own printing precision is published as a win, and a second live surface already says it is not.** `campaign/BOARD_RESCORE_2026-08-14.md` section 3.3 counts **5/8 cases won** against Wu & Zhang. | D134 | **This lab's mandated commit form structurally cannot set a file mode, so `sdk/tests/test_exec_bits.py` offers new files a repair no protocol-conforming agent can perform — and its own failure message names that impossible repair first.** `.git/config` carries `core.filemode = false`, which is correct for this box, and under it a PARTIAL commit (`git commit -F <msg> -- <paths>` — the only form every dispatch brief in this lab permits, precisely so that another agent's staged work cannot be swept in) **discards the index's mode and re-records `100644`**. Measured 2026-08-15 in a scratch repository with `core.filemode false`, four variants on one shebang-bearing file, and only the last works: **(A)** `git update-index --chmod=+x` then a pathspec commit → git reports *"nothing to commit"* and the mode is unchanged; **(B)** `--chmod=+x` plus a content change, pathspec commit → new blob, mode still `100644`; **(C)** `git add`, then `--chmod=+x`, then pathspec commit → the index reads `100755`, `git ls-tree HEAD` reads `100644`; **(D)** `git add`, `--chmod=+x`, then an index commit with **no pathspec** → `100755`. This is not theoretical: it happened in this pass. `scripts/check_rung_attribution.py` was `--chmod=+x`'d before `44ac957a` and that commit recorded `100644` anyway, and a second attempt to fix it produced *"nothing to commit, working tree clean"*. `test_exec_bits.py` was right both times — it reads `git ls-tree -r HEAD` on purpose, because its own first draft read `git ls-files -s` and passed on a mode that was staged and never committed (`0462b45b`). **The consequence for the register: under this protocol `WAIVED_NO_EXEC_BIT` can only ever grow.** Every new shebang-bearing file is offered two repairs, one of which the protocol forbids and the other of which is the waiver, so the waiver is not a grandfathering boundary that shrinks — it is the only exit. That is a different object from what the module docstring describes ("the register makes the gap countable and stops it growing"): it still stops the gap growing *silently*, but it cannot stop it growing. | Found 2026-08-15 while landing the attribution mechanism (`e933e31b`, anchored `44ac957a`); the four-variant measurement is a scratch repository under `/tmp`, `git init` with `core.filemode false`, one file, `git ls-tree HEAD` read after each commit. Not inferred from documentation. **Cannot see:** whether any of the 234 files already in the register got there this way rather than by never having been chmod'd — this row measures the mechanism, not its history; and whether `core.filemode = false` is still needed on this box, which is the question that would dissolve the problem rather than route around it. | Three options, and the choice is the chief's because it is a protocol rule. **(a) Amend the commit protocol** to permit exactly one extra step for a mode change — `git update-index --chmod=+x <path>` followed by an index commit with the same single path staged — and say when it is allowed, since the reason for the pathspec rule (never sweep another agent's staged work) is real and a bare index commit defeats it. **(b) Set `core.filemode = true`** on this box, after which `git add` carries the mode and the ordinary pathspec commit works; needs a check that no tracked file's mode flips as a side effect. **(c) Accept the register as permanent** and say so in `exec_bits.py`'s docstring, replacing "stops it growing" with what is true. Until one is chosen, `test_exec_bits.py`'s failure message should stop leading with `git update-index --chmod=+x`, because following it produces a green index and a red test. Immediate and already done: `scripts/check_rung_attribution.py` is registered with the reason, and `scripts/check_normative_clauses.py` (landed today at `1c4a643f`, a different family, not touched here) is still red on the same test for the same reason. | chief (the protocol rule); the infra family (`exec_bits.py`'s docstring and message); the owner of `check_normative_clauses.py` |
| D135 | **A certificate serial identifies a BUILD, not a result: two renders of the same mission one second apart carry two different certificate numbers, and the tracked shipping bundle has already shipped seven of them for one airliner result.** `sdk/chief_engineer/certificate.py:863` `_human_number()` returns `C-{year}-{int(seal[:6],16) % 10000:04d}`. That IS deterministic from the seal, exactly as the module docstring and `demo-output/website/certificates/README.md:14` say. The defect is one level up: the seal covers `issued_utc` (`_seal_payload`, `certificate.py:551`), and **all 16 of its call sites in the acts, across 14 workflow modules, pass `issued_utc=time.strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', time.gmtime())`** -- the wall clock at render time, which is not a fact of the run. So the serial is a pure function of the clock, and the number is a 4-digit lossy projection of a hash that carries no information the seal does not already carry. MEASURED, not inferred. The formula re-derives all five serials in the shipped bundle from their own printed seals (`aeccc6` -> 5686, `4979bf` -> 5295, `fac49a` -> 4330, `6dd22c` -> 7228, `d88946` -> 0918). Executed in a scratch path with identical facts and only the clock moving, nine timestamps of the same result produced **nine distinct serials**. The 702 `certificate.ready` events in `sdk/chief-engineer-runs/mission-state/` carry **670 distinct serials**, i.e. very nearly one identity per build: `aircraft-optimization` alone has issued **96**, `geometry-study` 91, `adjoint-optimization` 90. The tracked zip's own history is the cleanest exhibit -- across nine commits of `dist/certonomous-demo.zip` the SAME airliner result shipped as C-2026-2843, 1557, 1218, 4533, 8701, 2027 and 5686, and the working tree now holds a further one (9575). **Second, independent defect in the same line: the namespace is only 10,000 wide and the draw is effectively random, so it collides.** 24 serials in the run logs have been issued to **two different missions** -- the birthday model for 702 draws from 10,000 predicts 24.6, and the observed count is 24, which is the signature of random assignment rather than identity. One collision is live right now: **C-2026-0105 is currently printed on `mission-output/supersonic-wedge/certificate.pdf` and was also issued to `adjoint-optimization-1785529216`.** Consequently **the question 'which serial is correct' is not well posed** -- there is no correct serial, because nothing anywhere binds a serial to a result. NOT REPAIRED, deliberately: every available repair changes an identity, and a regeneration that changes an identity is a second identity, not a fix. | D118's repaired bundle-drift check (`e01dfdf6`) flagged `mission-output/aircraft-optimization/certificate.pdf` as diverging from the tracked zip; investigated 2026-08-15 by decompressing the zip (`unzip -d`) and reading the PDFs' uncompressed content streams, since the streams are plain text but the zip is DEFLATE, so a byte sweep over the archive finds nothing. Byte-diff of the two aircraft certificates: 77 bytes differ, at exactly three sites -- the issuance timestamp, the two halves of the SHA-256 seal, and the two occurrences of the serial. **Every reported number, every channel, every word is identical**, so the two files record the same result and disagree only about who they say they are. Generator executed in `/tmp` scratch with `__pycache__` purged first; no solver run, no scoring call. | **The owner's decision, and it is a design decision, not a repair.** Three options. **(a) Drop the serial**: the seal already is the identity, it is collision-free, and it is already what the control room displays -- the number adds only collisions. **(b) Make the serial a function of the RESULT**: seal over the run's facts with `issued_utc` excluded, so re-issuing the same result reproduces the same number and a genuine change moves it; costs a widened namespace to survive collisions and re-issues every certificate once, at a known moment, on purpose. **(c) Keep the clock and add a register**: a tracked append-only serial -> (mission, seal, issued) map, which makes a cited serial resolvable forever and makes collisions detectable at issue time. (b) is the only option under which the word 'certificate' means what a reader takes it to mean. Whichever is chosen, D137 has to be answered in the same breath, because the published claim is the reader-facing half of this one. | chief (design decision); the certificate family; the `dist/` owner |
| D136 | **The shipped bundle pairs a FROZEN recording with a MUTABLE artifact path, so the control room displays one evidence seal on camera and the certificate it links to carries a different one -- live in every bundle shipped since 2026-08-10, on Act 1.** `scripts/build_laptop_bundle.py:167-193` picks a recorded mission (a fixed historical `events.jsonl` out of `sdk/chief-engineer-runs/mission-state/`), then for each artifact that recording references copies **the file living at `mission-output/<dir>/certificate.pdf` right now**. Historical bundles read with `git show <sha>:dist/certonomous-demo.zip` piped to `unzip`, over the nine commits that touch the tracked zip. | **The pairing rule, not the instance.** The bundle must either (a) copy the certificate the RECORDING references rather than whatever occupies the path today -- which requires the certificate to be archived per run instead of overwritten at one fixed path, i.e. it requires D135 to be settled first -- or (b) **verify the pair at build time and refuse to build when the event's `hash` does not equal the seal in the PDF it is about to ship**. (b) is cheap, is a strict improvement whatever is decided about D135, and would have caught this on 2026-08-10. | D137 | **Four tracked, published certificates print 'Reproducible from the sealed evidence bundle' on the sealed page, a camera beat is scripted to say it out loud, and every serial cited on a tracked surface -- 5 of 5 -- no longer resolves to anything.** The sentence 'Reproducible from the sealed evidence bundle; any change to the recorded run invalidates the seal.' is rendered on `demo-output/acts/round3/airliner_certificate.pdf`, `naca_certificate.pdf`, `valve_certificate.pdf` and `demo-output/website/certificates/b52-certificate-redesign.pdf` (all tracked), is described as the provenance footer in `demo-output/website/certificates/README.md:22` and `sdk/scripts/build_certificate_compare.py:90`, and is **beat 5 of a shoot**: `demo-output/website/motorbike-video/shotlist.md:47` reads '- Reproducible from the sealed evidence bundle.' under trigger `certificate.ready`. **The sentence has two readings and the page does not say which.** As VERIFICATION -- recompute the hash from the recorded facts and check it -- it is true, but only because the issuance timestamp it covers is printed on the certificate itself. As RE-ISSUANCE, which is what 'reproducible' means to a reader holding a credential, **it is false**: by D135 the evidence bundle does not determine the seal, because `issued_utc` is the wall clock and not a fact of the run, so re-issuing the same result reproduces neither the seal nor the number. On a credential shown to a buyer, the second reading is the one that lands. **The dangling half is measured across all three arms of the tree and is unanimous.** Of 20,688 TRACKED files, 33 hits name a serial under `git grep -I` **and that arm is itself defective, exactly as D139 says**: `-I` honours `.gitattributes` `binary`, which hid **precisely the four tracked published certificate PDFs** named above -- `git grep -a` reaches them and adds their own serials (7069, 7080, 8945 and the b52 redesign's 4658). They are self-identification, not citation, so no conclusion below moves; the four were found by a separate route. Re-run with `-a`, every distinct serial CITED by a tracked surface is still dead: **C-2026-9704** (`demo-output/plots/monte_carlo/panel_stats.md:15,65,74` and `sdk/scripts/render_mc_convergence.py:19,316,331,592,638`, cited as the race-study certificate publishing peak L/D 18.14 +-0.06 -- the live race-study certificate is **C-2026-7228**), **C-2026-7080** (airliner) and **C-2026-7069** (NACA 4412) and **C-2026-8945** (valve), all in `demo-output/acts/round3/VERIFICATION.md`, `summary.json`, the `*_certificate.txt` pages and `docs/HANDOFF-BACKEND3.md:86-88`, and **C-2026-4461** (`demo-output/plots/submarine_sail/events.jsonl:237`). Of 2 UNTRACKED-not-ignored files, none cite a serial. Of 37,253 GITIGNORED files (`git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard`), 720 cite one, essentially all of them per-run event logs. Against the 15 serials that resolve to a certificate PDF existing anywhere on this box right now, **the tracked score is 0 of 5**. **This is structural, not an accident of staleness**: there is no register -- nothing in the tree maps a serial to a mission, a seal or a file -- and `withdraw_certificate()` deliberately DELETES the previous page before each run, so the lab's own withdrawal doctrine ('nothing out of date is served', pinned by `sdk/tests/test_certificate_withdrawal.py`) guarantees that every previously cited serial becomes unresolvable. A citation defect this lab has repaired twice is here regenerated by design. **What does NOT depend on these, checked and reported as a negative so the blast radius is not overstated:** the credentials wall carries **no serial at all** -- the shipped `snapshot/credentials.json` keys its four bodies on geometry name, measured Cd and tier, so the sales artifact is untouched by D135 and D136. **The competition submission carries no certificate either** -- `git grep -i certificate` over `closure_challenge_submission_round5/`, `_round4/` and `closure_challenge_submission/` returns zero hits, so none of this is submission-facing and submissions stay PARKED regardless. The exposure is camera-facing only, which is also where it is worst. **The suite already half-knows**: `sdk/tests/test_certificate.py:259-263` BANS the string 'reproducible evidence' from ever rendering on a sealed page -- and the shipped footer reads 'Reproducible from the sealed evidence **bundle**', so the guard is defeated by one word of separation and the banned idea ships anyway. And **nothing in the repo ASSERTS certificate stability**: `sdk/tests/test_certificate.py:130` asserts only the FORMAT (`assertRegex(out['certificate_no'], r'^C-\d{4}-\d{4}$')`) and never that two builds agree, so no test fails today and none would fail under any of D135's options. The false statement is on the published page, not in the suite. | D139 | **`.gitattributes` marks 981 tracked files `binary`, and `git grep -I` -- the first arm of every sweep in this lab -- therefore skips 12 files that contain no NUL byte at all, five of them sealed product certificates whose claim text is plain uncompressed ASCII.** git's binary decision comes from the attribute, not from the bytes: `*.pdf`, `*.png`, `*.jpg`, `*.stl`, `*.obj` are all marked `binary` in `.gitattributes`. Measured at `44ac957a`, 2026-08-15T18:58Z, over `git ls-files` with `git check-attr binary` and a NUL test on the first 8,000 bytes: **981 marked, 12 of them actually text** -- `demo-output/website/certificates/b52-certificate-{current,redesign}.pdf`, `demo-output/acts/round3/{airliner,naca,valve}_certificate.pdf`, `docs/papers/Paper3.pdf`, four `.stl` and two `.obj`. Controls both ways per L-84: **positive** -- `command grep -c '0\.056647' demo-surfaces/motorBike.obj` returns 3 while `git grep -lI` on the same path exits 1 and `git grep -lI --text` recovers it; **must-not-match** -- the same literal in `docs/PRODUCT_LIST.md` is found by `git grep -lI` at exit 0, so the miss is specific to the attribute and not a broken pattern; **boundary** -- `demo-output/Gui_issue.png` carries a NUL in its first 200 bytes and must stay skipped, which scopes the finding to exactly the 12 and no wider. Materiality is the certificates: `command grep -aoE '\(([^)]{3,60})\) *Tj'` on `b52-certificate-current.pdf` returns `(CERTONOMOUS)`, `(CERTIFICATE OF AUTONOMOUS SOLVE)`, `(Mission geometry-study-b52)` -- the lab's most claim-bearing artefact class is plain-text greppable by GNU grep and invisible to every `git grep -I` sweep in this repository. This is a **second, independent** blind spot from L-75's: L-75 is about `.gitignore` and the shell's ugrep; this one survives `command grep` and bites the `git grep` arm that L-75's repair recommends. | Found 2026-08-15 while writing `docs/USING_THIS_LAB.md`; frame is tracked files only at `44ac957a`, live working tree, `git check-attr -z binary` over `git ls-files -z`, text defined as no NUL in the first 8,000 bytes. Filed at D139 and not D135: D135 was free when the highest ID was checked at 19:03Z and taken by a concurrent session before the row could be written, so this row takes the next free ID per the docket's collision rule rather than moving anyone. **Cannot see:** whether any past sweep actually missed a live claim in those 12 -- this measures the mechanism, not its history; whether compressed PDFs elsewhere carry claims that `--text` would still miss, since compression defeats grep regardless of the mark; and the same class of mark in the gitignored and out-of-repo arms, which `git grep` never reaches at all. | Zero compute. Two candidate repairs, and the choice is a method question rather than a code one. **(a)** Make `--text` the house form for the tracked arm of any completeness sweep, and say so wherever the three-arm sweep is written down -- cheapest, and it costs only speed. **(b)** Narrow `.gitattributes` so that only formats that are actually binary carry the mark, e.g. drop `*.stl` and `*.obj` (both are ASCII here) and keep it for `*.png`/`*.jpg`; this needs a check that no diff or EOL conversion breaks as a side effect, which is the reason the marks were added. Neither repairs the PDFs, which are marked correctly and are text only by accident of how they were generated. | fleet (the sweep form); chief (whether `.gitattributes` changes, since it affects diffs lab-wide) |
